n28 src/termio/Thread.zig:289-375
  289  fn drainMailbox(
  290      self: *Thread,
  291      cb: *CallbackData,
  292  ) !void {
  293      // We assert when starting the thread that this is the state
  294      const mailbox = cb.io.mailbox.spsc.queue;
  295      const io = cb.io;
  296      const data = &cb.data;
  297  
  298      // If we're draining, we just drain the mailbox and return.
  299      if (self.flags.drain) {
  300          while (mailbox.pop(global.io())) |msg| msg.deinit();
  301          return;
  302      }
  303  
  304      // This holds the mailbox lock for the duration of the drain. The
  305      // expectation is that all our message handlers will be non-blocking
  306      // ENOUGH to not mess up throughput on producers.
  307      var redraw: bool = false;
  308      while (mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
  309          // If we have a message we always redraw
  310          redraw = true;
  311  
  312          log.debug("mailbox message={s}", .{@tagName(message)});
  313          switch (message) {
  314              .color_scheme_report => |v| try io.colorSchemeReport(data, v.force),
  315              .visibility_report => |v| try io.visibilityReport(
  316                  data,
  317                  v.visible,
  318                  v.force,
  319              ),
  320              .crash => @panic("crash request, crashing intentionally"),
  321              .change_config => |config| {
  322                  defer config.alloc.destroy(config.ptr);
  323                  try io.changeConfig(data, config.ptr);
  324              },
  325              .inspector => |v| self.flags.has_inspector = v,
  326              .resize => |v| self.handleResize(cb, v),
  327              .size_report => |v| try io.sizeReport(data, v),
  328              .clear_screen => |v| try io.clearScreen(data, v.history),
  329              .scroll_viewport => |v| io.scrollViewport(v),
  330              .selection_scroll => |v| {
  331                  if (v) {
  332                      self.startScrollTimer(cb);
  333                  } else {
  334                      self.stopScrollTimer();
  335                  }
  336              },
  337              .jump_to_prompt => |v| try io.jumpToPrompt(v),
  338              .kitty_clipboard_grant_read => |v| {
  339                  defer v.alloc.free(v.pw);
  340                  try io.kittyClipboardGrant(v.pw, .read);
  341              },
  342              .kitty_clipboard_grant_write => |v| {
  343                  defer v.alloc.free(v.pw);
  344                  try io.kittyClipboardGrant(v.pw, .write);
  345              },
  346              .start_synchronized_output => self.startSynchronizedOutput(cb),
  347              .linefeed_mode => |v| self.flags.linefeed_mode = v,
  348              .focused => |v| try io.focusGained(data, v),
  349              .write_small => |v| try io.queueWrite(
  350                  data,
  351                  v.data[0..v.len],
  352                  self.flags.linefeed_mode,
  353              ),
  354              .write_stable => |v| try io.queueWrite(
  355                  data,
  356                  v,
  357                  self.flags.linefeed_mode,
  358              ),
  359              .write_alloc => |v| {
  360                  defer v.alloc.free(v.data);
  361                  try io.queueWrite(
  362                      data,
  363                      v.data,
  364                      self.flags.linefeed_mode,
  365                  );
  366              },
  367          }
  368      }
  369  
  370      // Trigger a redraw after we've drained so we don't waste cyces
  371      // messaging a redraw.
  372      if (redraw) {
  373          try io.renderer_wakeup.notify();
  374      }
  375  }
n28 src/termio/Thread.zig:389-406
  389  fn handleResize(self: *Thread, cb: *CallbackData, resize: renderer.Size) void {
  390      self.coalesce_data.resize = resize;
  391  
  392      // If the timer is already active we just return. In the future we want
  393      // to reset the timer up to a maximum wait time but for now this ensures
  394      // relatively smooth resizing.
  395      if (self.coalesce_c.state() == .active) return;
  396  
  397      self.coalesce.reset(
  398          &self.loop,
  399          &self.coalesce_c,
  400          &self.coalesce_cancel_c,
  401          Coalesce.min_ms,
  402          CallbackData,
  403          cb,
  404          coalesceCallback,
  405      );
  406  }
n28 src/termio/Thread.zig:427-471
  427  fn coalesceCallback(
  428      cb_: ?*CallbackData,
  429      _: *xev.Loop,
  430      _: *xev.Completion,
  431      r: xev.Timer.RunError!void,
  432  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  433      _ = r catch |err| switch (err) {
  434          error.Canceled => {},
  435          else => {
  436              log.warn("error during coalesce callback err={}", .{err});
  437              return .disarm;
  438          },
  439      };
  440  
  441      const cb = cb_ orelse return .disarm;
  442  
  443      if (cb.self.coalesce_data.resize) |v| {
  444          cb.self.coalesce_data.resize = null;
  445          cb.io.resize(&cb.data, v) catch |err| {
  446              log.warn("error during resize err={}", .{err});
  447          };
  448      }
  449  
  450      return .disarm;
  451  }
  452  
  453  fn wakeupCallback(
  454      cb_: ?*CallbackData,
  455      _: *xev.Loop,
  456      _: *xev.Completion,
  457      r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
  458  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  459      _ = r catch |err| {
  460          log.err("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
  461          return .rearm;
  462      };
  463  
  464      // When we wake up, we check the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
  465      // wake up our thread after publishing.
  466      const cb = cb_ orelse return .rearm;
  467      cb.self.drainMailbox(cb) catch |err|
  468          log.err("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
  469  
  470      return .rearm;
  471  }
n28 src/termio/Termio.zig:437-463
  437  pub fn queueMessage(
  438      self: *Termio,
  439      msg: termio.Message,
  440      mutex: MutexState,
  441  ) void {
  442      self.mailbox.send(msg, switch (mutex) {
  443          .locked => self.renderer_state.mutex,
  444          .unlocked => null,
  445      });
  446      self.mailbox.notify();
  447  }
  448  
  449  /// Queue a write directly to the pty.
  450  ///
  451  /// If you're using termio.Thread, this must ONLY be called from the
  452  /// mailbox thread. If you're not on the thread, use queueMessage with
  453  /// mailbox messages instead.
  454  ///
  455  /// If you're not using termio.Thread, this is not threadsafe.
  456  pub inline fn queueWrite(
  457      self: *Termio,
  458      td: *ThreadData,
  459      data: []const u8,
  460      linefeed: bool,
  461  ) !void {
  462      try self.backend.queueWrite(self.alloc, td, data, linefeed);
  463  }
n28 src/termio/Termio.zig:524-564
  524  pub fn resize(
  525      self: *Termio,
  526      td: *ThreadData,
  527      size: renderer.Size,
  528  ) !void {
  529      self.size = size;
  530      const grid_size = size.grid();
  531  
  532      // Enter the critical area that we want to keep small
  533      {
  534          self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  535          defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  536  
  537          // Resizing the pty can immediately wake the shell with SIGWINCH.
  538          // Hold the same lock used by processOutput before notifying it, so
  539          // its redraw cannot be parsed against the old terminal dimensions.
  540          try self.backend.resize(grid_size, size.terminal());
  541  
  542          // Update the size of our terminal state
  543          try self.terminal.resize(
  544              self.alloc,
  545              .{
  546                  .cols = grid_size.columns,
  547                  .rows = grid_size.rows,
  548                  .cell_size_px = .{
  549                      .width = self.size.cell.width,
  550                      .height = self.size.cell.height,
  551                  },
  552              },
  553          );
  554  
  555          // If we have size reporting enabled we need to send a report.
  556          if (self.terminal.modes.get(.in_band_size_reports)) {
  557              try self.sizeReportLocked(td, .mode_2048);
  558          }
  559      }
  560  
  561      // Mail the renderer so that it can update the GPU and re-render
  562      _ = self.renderer_mailbox.push(global.io(), .{ .resize = size }, .{ .forever = {} });
  563      self.renderer_wakeup.notify() catch {};
  564  }

n28 src/termio/Exec.zig:403-490
  403  pub fn queueWrite(
  404      self: *Exec,
  405      alloc: Allocator,
  406      td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData,
  407      data: []const u8,
  408      linefeed: bool,
  409  ) !void {
  410      _ = self;
  411      const exec = &td.backend.exec;
  412  
  413      // If our process is exited then we don't send any more writes.
  414      if (exec.exited) return;
  415  
  416      // We go through and chunk the data if necessary to fit into
  417      // our cached buffers that we can queue to the stream.
  418      var i: usize = 0;
  419      while (i < data.len) {
  420          const w = try exec.write_pool.create(alloc);
  421          w.td = exec;
  422          const buf = &w.buf;
  423          const slice = slice: {
  424              // The maximum end index is either the end of our data or
  425              // the end of our buffer, whichever is smaller.
  426              const max = @min(data.len, i + buf.len);
  427  
  428              // Fast
  429              if (!linefeed) {
  430                  fastmem.copy(u8, buf, data[i..max]);
  431                  const len = max - i;
  432                  i = max;
  433                  break :slice buf[0..len];
  434              }
  435  
  436              // Slow, have to replace \r with \r\n
  437              var buf_i: usize = 0;
  438              while (i < data.len and buf_i < buf.len - 1) {
  439                  const ch = data[i];
  440                  i += 1;
  441  
  442                  if (ch != '\r') {
  443                      buf[buf_i] = ch;
  444                      buf_i += 1;
  445                      continue;
  446                  }
  447  
  448                  // CRLF
  449                  buf[buf_i] = '\r';
  450                  buf[buf_i + 1] = '\n';
  451                  buf_i += 2;
  452              }
  453  
  454              break :slice buf[0..buf_i];
  455          };
  456  
  457          //for (slice) |b| log.warn("write: {x}", .{b});
  458  
  459          exec.write_stream.queueWrite(
  460              td.loop,
  461              &exec.write_queue,
  462              &w.req,
  463              .{ .slice = slice },
  464              ThreadData.Write,
  465              w,
  466              ttyWrite,
  467          );
  468      }
  469  }
  470  
  471  fn ttyWrite(
  472      w_: ?*ThreadData.Write,
  473      _: *xev.Loop,
  474      _: *xev.Completion,
  475      _: xev.Stream,
  476      _: xev.WriteBuffer,
  477      r: xev.WriteError!usize,
  478  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  479      const w = w_.?;
  480      w.td.write_pool.destroy(w);
  481  
  482      const d = r catch |err| {
  483          log.err("write error: {}", .{err});
  484          return .disarm;
  485      };
  486      _ = d;
  487      //log.info("WROTE: {d}", .{d});
  488  
  489      return .disarm;
  490  }

n29 src/renderer/State.zig:13-52
   13  /// The mutex that must be held while reading any of the data in the
   14  /// members of this state. Note that the state itself is NOT protected
   15  /// by the mutex and is NOT thread-safe, only the members values of the
   16  /// state (i.e. the terminal, devmode, etc. values).
   17  mutex: *std.Io.Mutex,
   18  
   19  /// The terminal data.
   20  terminal: *terminalpkg.Terminal,
   21  
   22  /// The terminal inspector, if any. This will be null while the inspector
   23  /// is not active and will be set when it is active.
   24  inspector: ?*Inspector = null,
   25  
   26  /// Dead key state. This will render the current dead key preedit text
   27  /// over the cursor. This currently only ever renders a single codepoint.
   28  /// Preedit can in theory be multiple codepoints long but that is left as
   29  /// a future exercise.
   30  preedit: ?Preedit = null,
   31  
   32  /// Mouse state. This only contains state relevant to what renderers
   33  /// need about the mouse.
   34  mouse: Mouse = .{},
   35  
   36  /// The number of threads currently waiting to acquire `mutex` via
   37  /// `lockDemand`. This is not protected by the mutex; it is read by
   38  /// hot lock/unlock loops (the IO parse thread) in `yieldToDemand` to
   39  /// decide whether to hand the mutex off before relocking it.
   40  demand: std.atomic.Value(u32) = .init(0),
   41  
   42  /// Handoff generation counter. Incremented (with a futex wake) by
   43  /// `unlockDemand` after a demanding waiter releases the mutex, so that
   44  /// `yieldToDemand` knows the waiter had its turn.
   45  handoff_gen: std.atomic.Value(u32) = .init(0),
   46  
   47  /// How long `yieldToDemand` sleeps waiting for a demanding waiter to
   48  /// take its turn before giving up. This bounds how long the IO parse
   49  /// thread can stall if a wake is lost or the waiter is descheduled; a
   50  /// demanding critical section (the renderer's frame snapshot) is
   51  /// microseconds, so one millisecond is generous.
   52  const handoff_timeout_ns = 1 * std.time.ns_per_ms;
n29 src/renderer/State.zig:67-105
   67  pub fn lockDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   68      _ = self.demand.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   69      self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   70      const prev = self.demand.fetchSub(1, .monotonic);
   71      assert(prev > 0);
   72  }
   73  
   74  /// Release `mutex` acquired via `lockDemand` and notify hot loops
   75  /// parked in `yieldToDemand` that the demanding waiter had its turn.
   76  pub fn unlockDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   77      self.mutex.unlock(io);
   78      _ = self.handoff_gen.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   79      io.futexWake(u32, &self.handoff_gen.raw, 1);
   80  }
   81  
   82  /// Called by hot lock/unlock loops between critical sections, with
   83  /// `mutex` NOT held: if a `lockDemand` waiter exists, sleep until it
   84  /// has acquired and released the mutex (or the timeout passes). This
   85  /// is the handoff that unfair mutexes never do on their own.
   86  ///
   87  /// The orderings here are all monotonic because these atomics are a
   88  /// scheduling heuristic, not a synchronization boundary: the mutex
   89  /// itself orders the protected data, and the timeout bounds any
   90  /// staleness.
   91  pub fn yieldToDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   92      if (self.demand.load(.monotonic) == 0) return;
   93  
   94      // Snapshot the generation before rechecking demand: if the waiter
   95      // acquires and releases between our check and the wait below, the
   96      // generation no longer matches and timedWait returns immediately.
   97      const gen = self.handoff_gen.load(.monotonic);
   98      if (self.demand.load(.monotonic) == 0) return;
   99      io.futexWaitTimeout(
  100          u32,
  101          &self.handoff_gen.raw,
  102          gen,
  103          .{ .duration = .{ .raw = .fromNanoseconds(handoff_timeout_ns), .clock = .awake } },
  104      ) catch {};
  105  }

 1523                          &self.terminal_state,
 1524                      );

 1526                  }

 1529                  // This requires terminal state because of URLs.

 1544                      };

 1548                      const insp = state.inspector orelse break :overlay &.{};
 1549                      const renderer_info = insp.rendererInfo();
 1550                      break :overlay renderer_info.overlayFeatures(
 1551                          arena_alloc,
 1552                      ) catch &.{};
 1553                  };
 1554

 1557                      .mouse = state.mouse,

n31 src/Surface.zig:2669-2738
 2669  pub fn preeditCallback(self: *Surface, preedit_: ?[]const u8) !void {
 2670      // log.debug("text preeditCallback value={any}", .{preedit_});
 2671  
 2672      // Crash metadata in case we crash in here
 2673      crash.sentry.thread_state = self.crashThreadState();
 2674      defer crash.sentry.thread_state = null;
 2675  
 2676      self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 2677      defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 2678  
 2679      // We clear our selection when ANY OF:
 2680      // 1. We have an existing preedit
 2681      // 2. We have preedit text
 2682      if (self.renderer_state.preedit != null or
 2683          preedit_ != null)
 2684      {
 2685          if (self.config.selection_clear_on_typing) {
 2686              self.setSelection(null) catch {};
 2687          }
 2688      }
 2689  
 2690      // We always clear our prior preedit
 2691      if (self.renderer_state.preedit) |p| {
 2692          self.alloc.free(p.codepoints);
 2693          self.renderer_state.preedit = null;
 2694      }
 2695  
 2696      // Mark preedit dirty flag
 2697      self.io.terminal.flags.dirty.preedit = true;
 2698  
 2699      // If we have no text, we're done. We queue a render in case we cleared
 2700      // a prior preedit (likely).
 2701      const text = preedit_ orelse {
 2702          try self.queueRender();
 2703          return;
 2704      };
 2705  
 2706      // We convert the UTF-8 text to codepoints.
 2707      const view = try std.unicode.Utf8View.init(text);
 2708      var it = view.iterator();
 2709  
 2710      // Allocate the codepoints slice
 2711      const Codepoint = rendererpkg.State.Preedit.Codepoint;
 2712      var codepoints: std.ArrayList(Codepoint) = .empty;
 2713      defer codepoints.deinit(self.alloc);
 2714      while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
 2715          const width: usize = @intCast(unicode.table.get(cp).width);
 2716  
 2717          // I've never seen a preedit text with a zero-width character. In
 2718          // theory its possible but we can't really handle it right now.
 2719          // Let's just ignore it.
 2720          if (width <= 0) continue;
 2721  
 2722          try codepoints.append(
 2723              self.alloc,
 2724              .{ .codepoint = cp, .wide = width >= 2 },
 2725          );
 2726      }
 2727  
 2728      // If we have no codepoints, then we're done.
 2729      if (codepoints.items.len == 0) {
 2730          try self.queueRender();
 2731          return;
 2732      }
 2733  
 2734      self.renderer_state.preedit = .{
 2735          .codepoints = try codepoints.toOwnedSlice(self.alloc),
 2736      };
 2737      try self.queueRender();
 2738  }

n32 src/renderer/State.zig:36-105
   36  /// The number of threads currently waiting to acquire `mutex` via
   37  /// `lockDemand`. This is not protected by the mutex; it is read by
   38  /// hot lock/unlock loops (the IO parse thread) in `yieldToDemand` to
   39  /// decide whether to hand the mutex off before relocking it.
   40  demand: std.atomic.Value(u32) = .init(0),
   41  
   42  /// Handoff generation counter. Incremented (with a futex wake) by
   43  /// `unlockDemand` after a demanding waiter releases the mutex, so that
   44  /// `yieldToDemand` knows the waiter had its turn.
   45  handoff_gen: std.atomic.Value(u32) = .init(0),
   46  
   47  /// How long `yieldToDemand` sleeps waiting for a demanding waiter to
   48  /// take its turn before giving up. This bounds how long the IO parse
   49  /// thread can stall if a wake is lost or the waiter is descheduled; a
   50  /// demanding critical section (the renderer's frame snapshot) is
   51  /// microseconds, so one millisecond is generous.
   52  const handoff_timeout_ns = 1 * std.time.ns_per_ms;
   53  
   54  /// Acquire `mutex` while signaling demand for it. Use this instead of
   55  /// locking the mutex directly on threads that must not be starved by
   56  /// a hot lock/unlock loop (the renderer's frame snapshot). Must be
   57  /// released with `unlockDemand`; releasing with `mutex.unlock` keeps
   58  /// the data safe but makes parked `yieldToDemand` callers wait out
   59  /// their full timeout.
   60  ///
   61  /// Both `std.Thread.Mutex` and os_unfair_lock are unfair: a running
   62  /// thread that unlocks and immediately relocks beats a sleeping
   63  /// waiter every time, because the waiter must first be woken and
   64  /// scheduled. Under sustained pty output the IO parse thread is
   65  /// exactly such a loop, so without this signal the renderer can
   66  /// starve for as long as the output lasts.
   67  pub fn lockDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   68      _ = self.demand.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   69      self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   70      const prev = self.demand.fetchSub(1, .monotonic);
   71      assert(prev > 0);
   72  }
   73  
   74  /// Release `mutex` acquired via `lockDemand` and notify hot loops
   75  /// parked in `yieldToDemand` that the demanding waiter had its turn.
   76  pub fn unlockDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   77      self.mutex.unlock(io);
   78      _ = self.handoff_gen.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   79      io.futexWake(u32, &self.handoff_gen.raw, 1);
   80  }
   81  
   82  /// Called by hot lock/unlock loops between critical sections, with
   83  /// `mutex` NOT held: if a `lockDemand` waiter exists, sleep until it
   84  /// has acquired and released the mutex (or the timeout passes). This
   85  /// is the handoff that unfair mutexes never do on their own.
   86  ///
   87  /// The orderings here are all monotonic because these atomics are a
   88  /// scheduling heuristic, not a synchronization boundary: the mutex
   89  /// itself orders the protected data, and the timeout bounds any
   90  /// staleness.
   91  pub fn yieldToDemand(self: *State, io: std.Io) void {
   92      if (self.demand.load(.monotonic) == 0) return;
   93  
   94      // Snapshot the generation before rechecking demand: if the waiter
   95      // acquires and releases between our check and the wait below, the
   96      // generation no longer matches and timedWait returns immediately.
   97      const gen = self.handoff_gen.load(.monotonic);
   98      if (self.demand.load(.monotonic) == 0) return;
   99      io.futexWaitTimeout(
  100          u32,
  101          &self.handoff_gen.raw,
  102          gen,
  103          .{ .duration = .{ .raw = .fromNanoseconds(handoff_timeout_ns), .clock = .awake } },
  104      ) catch {};
  105  }

  707      // the lock to grab our read data.
  708      self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  709      defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  710      self.processOutputLocked(buf);
  711  }

  712          pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, options: renderer.Options) !Self {
  713              // Initialize our graphics API wrapper, this will prepare the
  714              // surface provided by the apprt and set up any API- and surface-
  715              // specific GPU resources.

  717                  alloc,

  722

  727                  .features = options.config.font_features.items,

  840  
  841          pub fn deinit(self: *Self) void {
  842              // This only deinitializes and frees CPU-side state
  843              // and does not free GPU resources like the swap chain and
  844              // shaders. Those are freed with `releaseGpuResources`.
  845  
  846              if (self.overlay) |*overlay| overlay.deinit(self.alloc);
  847              self.terminal_state.deinit(self.alloc);
  848              if (self.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  849              if (self.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  850  
  851              self.latest_frame.deinit(global.io());
  852  
  853              // Stop and release the display link. Surfaces are destroyed
  854              // on the main thread, where `close` stops the link before
  855              // returning. After this the display link callback is never
  856              // invoked again, so `renderer.Thread` can free the `draw_now`
  857              // handle the callback points at.
  858              if (DisplayLink != void) {
  859                  if (self.display_link) |display_link| {
  860                      display_link.close();
  861                      self.display_link = null;
  862                  }
  863              }
  864  
  865              self.cells.deinit(self.alloc);
  866  
  867              self.font_shaper.deinit();
  868              self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
  869  
  870              self.config.deinit();
  871              self.api.deinit();
  872  
  873              self.* = undefined;
  874          }

n33 src/renderer/generic.zig:1716-1731
 1716          pub fn drawFrame(
 1717              self: *Self,
 1718              sync: bool,
 1719          ) !void {
 1720              // Everything that touches draw state happens under the draw
 1721              // mutex. The display link is synced after the mutex is
 1722              // released. It doesn't need the lock, and this keeps the
 1723              // critical section to just drawing.
 1724              const sync_display_link = locked: {
 1725                  self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1726                  defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1727                  break :locked try self.drawFrameLocked(sync);
 1728              };
 1729  
 1730              if (sync_display_link) self.syncDisplayLink(null, null);
 1731          }

n34 src/font/SharedGridSet.zig:96-154
   96  pub fn ref(
   97      self: *SharedGridSet,
   98      config: *const DerivedConfig,
   99      font_size: DesiredSize,
  100  ) !struct { Key, *SharedGrid } {
  101      var key = try Key.init(self.alloc, config, font_size);
  102      errdefer key.deinit();
  103  
  104      self.lock.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  105      defer self.lock.unlock(global.io());
  106  
  107      const gop = try self.map.getOrPut(self.alloc, key);
  108      if (gop.found_existing) {
  109          log.debug("found cached grid for font config", .{});
  110  
  111          // We can deinit the key because we found a cached value.
  112          key.deinit();
  113  
  114          // Increment our ref count and return the cache
  115          gop.value_ptr.ref += 1;
  116          return .{ gop.key_ptr.*, gop.value_ptr.grid };
  117      }
  118      errdefer self.map.removeByPtr(gop.key_ptr);
  119  
  120      log.debug("initializing new grid for font config", .{});
  121  
  122      // A new font config, initialize the cache.
  123      const grid = try self.alloc.create(SharedGrid);
  124      errdefer self.alloc.destroy(grid);
  125      gop.value_ptr.* = .{
  126          .grid = grid,
  127          .ref = 1,
  128      };
  129  
  130      grid.* = try .init(self.alloc, resolver: {
  131          // Build our collection. This is the expensive operation that
  132          // involves finding fonts, loading them (maybe, some are deferred),
  133          // etc.
  134          var c = try self.collection(&key, font_size, config);
  135          errdefer c.deinit(self.alloc);
  136  
  137          // Setup our enabled/disabled styles
  138          var styles = CodepointResolver.StyleStatus.initFill(true);
  139          styles.set(.bold, config.@"font-style-bold" != .false);
  140          styles.set(.italic, config.@"font-style-italic" != .false);
  141          styles.set(.bold_italic, config.@"font-style-bold-italic" != .false);
  142  
  143          // Init our resolver which just requires setting fields.
  144          break :resolver .{
  145              .collection = c,
  146              .styles = styles,
  147              .discover = try self.discover(),
  148              .codepoint_map = key.codepoint_map,
  149          };
  150      });
  151      errdefer grid.deinit(self.alloc);
  152  
  153      return .{ gop.key_ptr.*, gop.value_ptr.grid };
  154  }
n34 src/font/SharedGridSet.zig:411-431
  411  /// Decrement the ref count for the given key. If the ref count is zero,
  412  /// the grid will be deinitialized and removed from the map.j:w
  413  pub fn deref(self: *SharedGridSet, key: Key) void {
  414      self.lock.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  415      defer self.lock.unlock(global.io());
  416  
  417      const entry = self.map.getEntry(key) orelse return;
  418      assert(entry.value_ptr.ref >= 1);
  419  
  420      // If we have more than one reference, decrement and return.
  421      if (entry.value_ptr.ref > 1) {
  422          entry.value_ptr.ref -= 1;
  423          return;
  424      }
  425  
  426      // We are at a zero ref count so deinit the group and remove.
  427      entry.key_ptr.deinit();
  428      entry.value_ptr.grid.deinit(self.alloc);
  429      self.alloc.destroy(entry.value_ptr.grid);
  430      self.map.removeByPtr(entry.key_ptr);
  431  }

n34 src/renderer/generic.zig:302-354
  302          /// Swap chain which maintains multiple copies of the state needed to
  303          /// render a frame, so that we can start building the next frame while
  304          /// the previous frame is still being processed on the GPU.
  305          const SwapChain = struct {
  306              // The count of buffers we use for double/triple buffering.
  307              // If this is one then we don't do any double+ buffering at all.
  308              // This is comptime because there isn't a good reason to change
  309              // this at runtime and there is a lot of complexity to support it.
  310              const buf_count = GraphicsAPI.swap_chain_count;
  311  
  312              /// `buf_count` structs that can hold the
  313              /// data needed by the GPU to draw a frame.
  314              frames: [buf_count]FrameState,
  315              /// Index of the most recently used frame state struct.
  316              frame_index: std.math.IntFittingRange(0, buf_count) = 0,
  317              /// Semaphore that we wait on to make sure we have an available
  318              /// frame state struct so we can start working on a new frame.
  319              frame_sema: std.Io.Semaphore = .{ .permits = buf_count },
  320  
  321              pub fn init(api: GraphicsAPI, custom_shaders: bool) !SwapChain {
  322                  var result: SwapChain = .{ .frames = undefined };
  323  
  324                  // Initialize all of our frame state.
  325                  for (&result.frames) |*frame| {
  326                      frame.* = try FrameState.init(api, custom_shaders);
  327                  }
  328  
  329                  return result;
  330              }
  331  
  332              pub fn deinit(self: *SwapChain) void {
  333                  // Wait for all of our inflight draws to complete
  334                  // so that we can cleanly deinit our GPU state.
  335                  for (0..buf_count) |_| self.frame_sema.waitUncancelable(
  336                      global.io(),
  337                  );
  338                  for (&self.frames) |*frame| frame.deinit();
  339              }
  340  
  341              /// Get the next frame state to draw to. This will wait on the
  342              /// semaphore to ensure that the frame is available. This must
  343              /// always be paired with a call to releaseFrame.
  344              pub fn nextFrame(self: *SwapChain) *FrameState {
  345                  self.frame_sema.waitUncancelable(global.io());
  346                  self.frame_index = (self.frame_index + 1) % buf_count;
  347                  return &self.frames[self.frame_index];
  348              }
  349  
  350              /// This should be called when the frame has completed drawing.
  351              pub fn releaseFrame(self: *SwapChain) void {
  352                  self.frame_sema.post(global.io());
  353              }
  354          };
n34 src/renderer/generic.zig:356-469
  356          /// State we need duplicated for every frame. Any state that could be
  357          /// in a data race between the GPU and CPU while a frame is being drawn
  358          /// should be in this struct.
  359          ///
  360          /// While a draw is in-process, we "lock" the state (via a semaphore)
  361          /// and prevent the CPU from updating the state until our graphics API
  362          /// reports that the frame is complete.
  363          ///
  364          /// This is used to implement double/triple buffering.
  365          const FrameState = struct {
  366              uniforms: UniformBuffer,
  367              cells: CellTextBuffer,
  368              cells_bg: CellBgBuffer,
  369  
  370              grayscale: Texture,
  371              grayscale_modified: usize = 0,
  372              color: Texture,
  373              color_modified: usize = 0,
  374  
  375              target: Target,
  376              /// See property of same name on Renderer for explanation.
  377              target_config_modified: usize = 0,
  378  
  379              /// Buffer with the vertex data for our background image.
  380              ///
  381              /// TODO: Make this an optional and only create it
  382              ///       if we actually have a background image.
  383              bg_image_buffer: BgImageBuffer,
  384              /// See property of same name on Renderer for explanation.
  385              bg_image_buffer_modified: usize = 0,
  386  
  387              /// Custom shader state, this is null if we have no custom shaders.
  388              custom_shader_state: ?CustomShaderState = null,
  389  
  390              const UniformBuffer = Buffer(shaderpkg.Uniforms);
  391              const CellBgBuffer = Buffer(shaderpkg.CellBg);
  392              const CellTextBuffer = Buffer(shaderpkg.CellText);
  393              const BgImageBuffer = Buffer(shaderpkg.BgImage);
  394  
  395              pub fn init(api: GraphicsAPI, custom_shaders: bool) !FrameState {
  396                  // Uniform buffer contains exactly 1 uniform struct. The
  397                  // uniform data will be undefined so this must be set before
  398                  // a frame is drawn.
  399                  var uniforms = try UniformBuffer.init(api.uniformBufferOptions(), 1);
  400                  errdefer uniforms.deinit();
  401  
  402                  // Create GPU buffers for our cells.
  403                  //
  404                  // We start them off with a size of 1, which will of course be
  405                  // too small, but they will be resized as needed. This is a bit
  406                  // wasteful but since it's a one-time thing it's not really a
  407                  // huge concern.
  408                  var cells = try CellTextBuffer.init(api.fgBufferOptions(), 1);
  409                  errdefer cells.deinit();
  410                  var cells_bg = try CellBgBuffer.init(api.bgBufferOptions(), 1);
  411                  errdefer cells_bg.deinit();
  412  
  413                  // Create a GPU buffer for our background image info.
  414                  var bg_image_buffer = try BgImageBuffer.init(
  415                      api.bgImageBufferOptions(),
  416                      1,
  417                  );
  418                  errdefer bg_image_buffer.deinit();
  419  
  420                  // Initialize our textures for our font atlas.
  421                  //
  422                  // As with the buffers above, we start these off as small
  423                  // as possible since they'll inevitably be resized anyway.
  424                  const grayscale = try api.initAtlasTexture(&.{
  425                      .data = undefined,
  426                      .size = 1,
  427                      .format = .grayscale,
  428                  });
  429                  errdefer grayscale.deinit();
  430                  const color = try api.initAtlasTexture(&.{
  431                      .data = undefined,
  432                      .size = 1,
  433                      .format = .bgra,
  434                  });
  435                  errdefer color.deinit();
  436  
  437                  var custom_shader_state =
  438                      if (custom_shaders)
  439                          try CustomShaderState.init(api)
  440                      else
  441                          null;
  442                  errdefer if (custom_shader_state) |*state| state.deinit();
  443  
  444                  // Initialize the target. Just as with the other resources,
  445                  // start it off as small as we can since it'll be resized.
  446                  const target = try api.initTarget(1, 1);
  447  
  448                  return .{
  449                      .uniforms = uniforms,
  450                      .cells = cells,
  451                      .cells_bg = cells_bg,
  452                      .bg_image_buffer = bg_image_buffer,
  453                      .grayscale = grayscale,
  454                      .color = color,
  455                      .target = target,
  456                      .custom_shader_state = custom_shader_state,
  457                  };
  458              }
  459  
  460              pub fn deinit(self: *FrameState) void {
  461                  self.target.deinit();
  462                  self.uniforms.deinit();
  463                  self.cells.deinit();
  464                  self.cells_bg.deinit();
  465                  self.grayscale.deinit();
  466                  self.color.deinit();
  467                  self.bg_image_buffer.deinit();
  468                  if (self.custom_shader_state) |*state| state.deinit();
  469              }

n34 src/renderer/generic.zig:841-874
  841          pub fn deinit(self: *Self) void {
  842              // This only deinitializes and frees CPU-side state
  843              // and does not free GPU resources like the swap chain and
  844              // shaders. Those are freed with `releaseGpuResources`.
  845  
  846              if (self.overlay) |*overlay| overlay.deinit(self.alloc);
  847              self.terminal_state.deinit(self.alloc);
  848              if (self.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  849              if (self.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  850  
  851              self.latest_frame.deinit(global.io());
  852  
  853              // Stop and release the display link. Surfaces are destroyed
  854              // on the main thread, where `close` stops the link before
  855              // returning. After this the display link callback is never
  856              // invoked again, so `renderer.Thread` can free the `draw_now`
  857              // handle the callback points at.
  858              if (DisplayLink != void) {
  859                  if (self.display_link) |display_link| {
  860                      display_link.close();
  861                      self.display_link = null;
  862                  }
  863              }
  864  
  865              self.cells.deinit(self.alloc);
  866  
  867              self.font_shaper.deinit();
  868              self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
  869  
  870              self.config.deinit();
  871              self.api.deinit();
  872  
  873              self.* = undefined;
  874          }
n34 src/renderer/generic.zig:1317-1358
 1317          pub fn setFontGrid(self: *Self, grid: *font.SharedGrid) void {
 1318              self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1319              defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1320  
 1321              // Update our grid
 1322              self.font_grid = grid;
 1323  
 1324              // Update all our textures so that they sync on the next frame.
 1325              // We can modify this without a lock because the GPU does not
 1326              // touch this data. A released swap chain is rebuilt with
 1327              // fresh frames that sync all textures on first use.
 1328              if (self.swap_chain) |*sc| for (&sc.frames) |*frame| {
 1329                  frame.grayscale_modified = 0;
 1330                  frame.color_modified = 0;
 1331              };
 1332  
 1333              // Get our metrics from the grid. This doesn't require a lock because
 1334              // the metrics are never recalculated.
 1335              const metrics = grid.metrics;
 1336              self.grid_metrics = metrics;
 1337  
 1338              // Reset our shaper cache. If our font changed (not just the size) then
 1339              // the data in the shaper cache may be invalid and cannot be used, so we
 1340              // always clear the cache just in case.
 1341              const font_shaper_cache = font.ShaperCache.init();
 1342              self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
 1343              self.font_shaper_cache = font_shaper_cache;
 1344  
 1345              // Update cell size.
 1346              self.size.cell = .{
 1347                  .width = metrics.cell_width,
 1348                  .height = metrics.cell_height,
 1349              };
 1350  
 1351              // Update relevant uniforms
 1352              self.updateFontGridUniforms();
 1353  
 1354              // Force a full rebuild, because cached rows may still reference
 1355              // an outdated atlas from the old grid and this can cause garbage
 1356              // to be rendered.
 1357              self.markDirty();
 1358          }
n34 src/renderer/generic.zig:1771-1783
 1771              // Get our swap chain, rebuilding it if it was released
 1772              // while we were hidden. Rebuilding is deferred to draw
 1773              // time because resource creation must happen somewhere
 1774              // our graphics API allows it (OpenGL requires a current
 1775              // context, which drawFrame guarantees).
 1776              const swap_chain: *SwapChain, const swap_chain_rebuilt: bool =
 1777                  if (self.swap_chain) |*sc| .{ sc, false } else rebuild: {
 1778                      self.swap_chain = try SwapChain.init(
 1779                          self.api,
 1780                          self.has_custom_shaders,
 1781                      );
 1782                      break :rebuild .{ &self.swap_chain.?, true };
 1783                  };

 1889              // If our font atlas changed, sync the texture data
 1890              texture: {
 1891                  const modified = self.font_grid.atlas_grayscale.modified.load(.monotonic);
 1892                  if (modified <= frame.grayscale_modified) break :texture;
 1893                  self.font_grid.lock.lockSharedUncancelable(global.io());
 1894                  defer self.font_grid.lock.unlockShared(global.io());
 1895                  frame.grayscale_modified = self.font_grid.atlas_grayscale.modified.load(.monotonic);
 1896                  try self.syncAtlasTexture(&self.font_grid.atlas_grayscale, &frame.grayscale);
 1897              }
 1898              texture: {
 1899                  const modified = self.font_grid.atlas_color.modified.load(.monotonic);
 1900                  if (modified <= frame.color_modified) break :texture;
 1901                  self.font_grid.lock.lockSharedUncancelable(global.io());
 1902                  defer self.font_grid.lock.unlockShared(global.io());
 1903                  frame.color_modified = self.font_grid.atlas_color.modified.load(.monotonic);
 1904                  try self.syncAtlasTexture(&self.font_grid.atlas_color, &frame.color);
 1905              }
 1906

 1908              var frame_ctx = try self.api.beginFrame(self, &frame.target);
 1909              defer frame_ctx.complete(sync);

n34 src/renderer/OpenGL.zig:47-96
   47  /// The shared render device.
   48  device: *const Device,
   49  
   50  egl_context: *gl.egl.Context,
   51  
   52  pub fn init(
   53      alloc: Allocator,
   54      device: *const Device,
   55      opts: rendererpkg.Options,
   56  ) !OpenGL {
   57      const display = device.display;
   58  
   59      // Bind to OpenGL API explicitly before we create the context,
   60      // as otherwise it might fallback to OpenGL ES
   61      try egl.bindApi(egl.c.EGL_OPENGL_API);
   62  
   63      // Create our context. Contexts are surface-scoped because a
   64      // context can only be current on one thread at a time.
   65      const context = egl.Context.create(display, device.config, null, &.{
   66          egl.c.EGL_CONTEXT_MAJOR_VERSION,       MIN_VERSION_MAJOR,
   67          egl.c.EGL_CONTEXT_MINOR_VERSION,       MIN_VERSION_MINOR,
   68          egl.c.EGL_CONTEXT_OPENGL_PROFILE_MASK, egl.c.EGL_CONTEXT_OPENGL_CORE_PROFILE_BIT,
   69      }) catch |err| {
   70          log.warn("failed to create EGL context err={}", .{err});
   71          return err;
   72      };
   73      errdefer context.destroy(display) catch {};
   74  
   75      display.makeCurrent(null, null, context) catch |err| {
   76          log.warn("failed to make EGL context current err={}", .{err});
   77          return err;
   78      };
   79  
   80      // Release current so that the main thread
   81      // doesn't hold onto the GL context forever.
   82      defer display.releaseCurrent();
   83  
   84      return .{
   85          .alloc = alloc,
   86          .blending = opts.config.blending,
   87          .device = device,
   88          .egl_context = context,
   89      };
   90  }
   91  
   92  pub fn deinit(self: *OpenGL) void {
   93      self.device.display.releaseCurrent();
   94      self.egl_context.destroy(self.device.display) catch {};
   95      self.* = undefined;
   96  }
n34 src/renderer/metal/Device.zig:17-66
   17  /// Options used when creating the Metal device.
   18  /// There are currently no options.
   19  pub const Options = struct {};
   20  
   21  /// MTLDevice
   22  device: objc.Object,
   23  
   24  /// The default storage mode to use for resources created with our device.
   25  ///
   26  /// This is based on whether the device is a discrete GPU or not, since
   27  /// discrete GPUs do not have unified memory and therefore do not support
   28  /// the "shared" storage mode, instead we have to use the "managed" mode.
   29  default_storage_mode: mtl.MTLResourceOptions.StorageMode,
   30  
   31  /// The maximum 2D texture width and height supported by the device.
   32  max_texture_size: u32,
   33  
   34  pub fn init(alloc: std.mem.Allocator, options: Options) !*Device {
   35      _ = options;
   36      const self = try alloc.create(Device);
   37      errdefer alloc.destroy(self);
   38  
   39      // Choose our MTLDevice.
   40      const device = try chooseDevice();
   41      errdefer device.release();
   42  
   43      // Grab metadata about the device.
   44      const default_storage_mode: mtl.MTLResourceOptions.StorageMode = switch (comptime builtin.target.os.tag) {
   45          // manage mode is not supported by iOS
   46          .ios => .shared,
   47          else => if (device.getProperty(bool, "hasUnifiedMemory")) .shared else .managed,
   48      };
   49      const max_texture_size = queryMaxTextureSize(device);
   50      log.debug(
   51          "device properties default_storage_mode={} max_texture_size={}",
   52          .{ default_storage_mode, max_texture_size },
   53      );
   54  
   55      self.* = .{
   56          .device = device,
   57          .default_storage_mode = default_storage_mode,
   58          .max_texture_size = max_texture_size,
   59      };
   60      return self;
   61  }
   62  
   63  pub fn deinit(self: *Device, alloc: std.mem.Allocator) void {
   64      self.device.release();
   65      alloc.destroy(self);
   66  }
n34 src/renderer/opengl/Device.zig:18-74
   18  /// Options used when creating the OpenGL device.
   19  pub const Options = struct {
   20      /// Render with the provided EGL display.
   21      /// Caller is responsible for managing the lifetime of the display.
   22      /// The default EGL display will be used when null.
   23      display: ?*egl.Display = null,
   24  };
   25  
   26  /// The EGL display.
   27  ///
   28  /// The lifecycle of this display is always managed externally, both in the
   29  /// cases where we were given a display as a config option, or we use the
   30  /// systemwide default display that is cleaned up by the OS.
   31  display: *egl.Display,
   32  
   33  /// The EGL config used to create surface renderer contexts. Chosen
   34  /// once here so that all contexts share compatible capabilities.
   35  config: *egl.Config,
   36  
   37  pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, options: Options) !*Device {
   38      const self = try alloc.create(Device);
   39      errdefer alloc.destroy(self);
   40  
   41      try egl.load();
   42  
   43      const display = options.display orelse egl.Display.initPlatform(
   44          egl.c.EGL_PLATFORM_SURFACELESS_MESA,
   45          egl.c.EGL_DEFAULT_DISPLAY,
   46          null,
   47      ) catch |err| {
   48          if (err == error.BadParameter) logUnsupportedPlatform();
   49          return err;
   50      };
   51  
   52      log.info("EGL vendor={s}", .{display.queryString(.vendor) orelse "(unknown)"});
   53      log.info("EGL extensions={s}", .{display.queryString(.extensions) orelse "(unknown)"});
   54  
   55      // Choose a config. We need a config that is renderable with
   56      // OpenGL and a RGBA8 color buffer.
   57      const config = egl.Config.choose(display, &.{
   58          egl.c.EGL_SURFACE_TYPE,    0,
   59          egl.c.EGL_RENDERABLE_TYPE, egl.c.EGL_OPENGL_BIT,
   60          egl.c.EGL_RED_SIZE,        8,
   61          egl.c.EGL_GREEN_SIZE,      8,
   62          egl.c.EGL_BLUE_SIZE,       8,
   63          egl.c.EGL_ALPHA_SIZE,      8,
   64      }) catch |err| {
   65          log.warn("failed to choose config err={}", .{err});
   66          return err;
   67      };
   68  
   69      self.* = .{
   70          .display = display,
   71          .config = config,
   72      };
   73      return self;
   74  }

n35 src/renderer/Thread.zig:113-257
  113  /// Initialize the thread. This does not START the thread. This only sets
  114  /// up all the internal state necessary prior to starting the thread. It
  115  /// is up to the caller to start the thread with the threadMain entrypoint.
  116  pub fn init(
  117      alloc: Allocator,
  118      config: *const configpkg.Config,
  119      surface: *apprt.Surface,
  120      renderer_impl: *rendererpkg.Renderer,
  121      state: *rendererpkg.State,
  122  ) !Thread {
  123      // Create our event loop.
  124      var loop = try xev.Loop.init(.{});
  125      errdefer loop.deinit();
  126  
  127      // This async handle is used to "wake up" the renderer and force a render.
  128      var wakeup_h = try xev.Async.init();
  129      errdefer wakeup_h.deinit();
  130  
  131      // This async handle is used to stop the loop and force the thread to end.
  132      var stop_h = try xev.Async.init();
  133      errdefer stop_h.deinit();
  134  
  135      // The primary timer for rendering.
  136      var render_h = try xev.Timer.init();
  137      errdefer render_h.deinit();
  138  
  139      // Draw now async, see comments.
  140      var draw_now = try xev.Async.init();
  141      errdefer draw_now.deinit();
  142  
  143      // Setup a timer for blinking the cursor
  144      var cursor_timer = try xev.Timer.init();
  145      errdefer cursor_timer.deinit();
  146  
  147      // The mailbox for messaging this thread
  148      var mailbox = try Mailbox.create(alloc);
  149      errdefer mailbox.destroy(alloc);
  150  
  151      var result: Thread = .{
  152          .alloc = alloc,
  153          .config = .init(config),
  154          .loop = loop,
  155          .wakeup = wakeup_h,
  156          .stop = stop_h,
  157          .render_h = render_h,
  158          .draw_now = draw_now,
  159          .cursor_h = cursor_timer,
  160          .surface = surface,
  161          .renderer = renderer_impl,
  162          .state = state,
  163          .mailbox = mailbox,
  164      };
  165  
  166      // Only enable compression if we have it enabled... save some
  167      // minor resources.
  168      if (comptime terminalpkg.compression_enabled) {
  169          result.compression = try .init();
  170      }
  171  
  172      return result;
  173  }
  174  
  175  /// Clean up the thread. This is only safe to call once the thread
  176  /// completes executing; the caller must join prior to this.
  177  pub fn deinit(self: *Thread) void {
  178      self.stop.deinit();
  179      self.wakeup.deinit();
  180      self.render_h.deinit();
  181      self.draw_now.deinit();
  182      self.cursor_h.deinit();
  183      if (comptime terminalpkg.compression_enabled)
  184          self.compression.deinit();
  185      self.loop.deinit();
  186  
  187      // Nothing can possibly access the mailbox anymore, destroy it.
  188      self.mailbox.destroy(self.alloc);
  189  }
  190  
  191  /// The main entrypoint for the thread.
  192  pub fn threadMain(self: *Thread) void {
  193      // Call child function so we can use errors...
  194      self.threadMain_() catch |err| {
  195          // In the future, we should expose this on the thread struct.
  196          log.warn("error in renderer err={}", .{err});
  197      };
  198  }
  199  
  200  fn threadMain_(self: *Thread) !void {
  201      defer log.debug("renderer thread exited", .{});
  202  
  203      // Right now, on Darwin, `std.Thread.setName` can only name the current
  204      // thread, and we have no way to get the current thread from within it,
  205      // so instead we use this code to name the thread instead.
  206      if (builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
  207          internal_os.macos.pthread_setname_np(&"renderer".*);
  208      }
  209  
  210      // Setup our crash metadata
  211      crash.sentry.thread_state = .{
  212          .type = .renderer,
  213          .surface = self.renderer.surface_mailbox.surface,
  214      };
  215      defer crash.sentry.thread_state = null;
  216  
  217      // Setup our thread QoS
  218      self.setQosClass();
  219  
  220      // Run our loop start/end callbacks if the renderer cares.
  221      const has_loop = @hasDecl(rendererpkg.Renderer, "loopEnter");
  222      if (has_loop) try self.renderer.loopEnter(self);
  223      defer if (has_loop) self.renderer.loopExit();
  224  
  225      // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This is important because some
  226      // renderers have to do per-thread setup. For example, OpenGL has to set
  227      // some thread-local state since that is how it works.
  228      try self.renderer.threadEnter(self.surface);
  229      defer self.renderer.threadExit();
  230  
  231      // Start the async handlers
  232      self.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, Thread, self, wakeupCallback);
  233      self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, Thread, self, stopCallback);
  234      self.draw_now.wait(&self.loop, &self.draw_now_c, Thread, self, drawNowCallback);
  235  
  236      // Send an initial wakeup message so that we render right away.
  237      try self.wakeup.notify();
  238  
  239      // Start blinking the cursor.
  240      self.cursor_h.run(
  241          &self.loop,
  242          &self.cursor_c,
  243          cursorBlinkInterval(),
  244          Thread,
  245          self,
  246          cursorTimerCallback,
  247      );
  248  
  249      // Arm the animation timer in case the renderer already needs
  250      // animation wakes (e.g. custom shaders loaded at startup).
  251      self.armAnimationTimer();
  252  
  253      // Run
  254      log.debug("starting renderer thread", .{});
  255      defer log.debug("starting renderer thread shutdown", .{});
  256      _ = try self.loop.run(.until_done);
  257  }

  727  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  728      _ = r catch unreachable;
  729      self_.?.loop.stop();
  730      return .disarm;
  731  }

  716      self.terminal_stream.handler.queueRender() catch unreachable;

n36 src/renderer/Thread.zig:290-303
  290  fn drainMailbox(self: *Thread) !void {
  291      // There's probably a more elegant way to do this...
  292      //
  293      // This is effectively an @autoreleasepool{} block, which we need in
  294      // order to ensure that autoreleased objects are properly released.
  295      const pool = if (builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin())
  296          @import("objc").AutoreleasePool.init()
  297      else
  298          void;
  299      defer if (builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) pool.deinit();
  300  
  301      while (self.mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
  302          log.debug("mailbox message={}", .{message});
  303          switch (message) {
n36 src/renderer/Thread.zig:306-350
  306              .visible => |v| visible: {
  307                  // If our state didn't change we do nothing.
  308                  if (self.flags.visible == v) break :visible;
  309  
  310                  // Set our visible state
  311                  self.flags.visible = v;
  312  
  313                  // Visibility affects our QoS class
  314                  self.setQosClass();
  315  
  316                  // If we became visible then we immediately rebuild cells
  317                  // (renderCallback skips updateFrame while invisible) and
  318                  // draw. Going through renderCallback also reschedules
  319                  // any Kitty graphics animation wakeup that lapsed
  320                  // while we were invisible.
  321                  if (v) _ = renderCallback(self, undefined, undefined, {});
  322  
  323                  // Notify the renderer so it can update any state.
  324                  self.renderer.setVisible(v);
  325  
  326                  // Note that we're explicitly today not stopping any
  327                  // cursor timers, draw timers, etc. These things have very
  328                  // little resource cost and properly maintaining their active
  329                  // state across different transitions is going to be bug-prone,
  330                  // so its easier to just let them keep firing and have them
  331                  // check the visible state themselves to control their behavior.
  332              },
  333  
  334              .focus => |v| focus: {
  335                  // If our state didn't change we do nothing.
  336                  if (self.flags.focused == v) break :focus;
  337  
  338                  // Set our state
  339                  self.flags.focused = v;
  340  
  341                  // Focus affects our QoS class
  342                  self.setQosClass();
  343  
  344                  // Set it on the renderer
  345                  try self.renderer.setFocus(v);
  346  
  347                  // Focus gates custom shader animation, so re-arm
  348                  // the animation timer for the new state.
  349                  self.armAnimationTimer();
  350

  382              .reset_cursor_blink => {

  384                  if (self.cursor_c.state() == .active) {
  385                      self.cursor_h.reset(
  386                          &self.loop,
  387                          &self.cursor_c,
  388                          &self.cursor_c_cancel,
  389                          cursorBlinkInterval(),
  390                          Thread,
  391                          self,
  392                          cursorTimerCallback,
  393                      );
  394                  }
  395              },
  396  
  397              .font_grid => |grid| {
  398                  self.renderer.setFontGrid(grid.grid);
  399                  grid.set.deref(grid.old_key);
  400              },
  401  
  402              .presentation_health => |v| self.renderer.setPresentationHealth(v),
  403

  405

  408                  defer config.alloc.destroy(config.impl);

  410                  try self.renderer.changeConfig(config.impl);

n36 src/renderer/Thread.zig:460-688
  460  /// Trigger a draw. This will not update frame data or anything, it will
  461  /// just trigger a draw/paint.
  462  fn drawFrame(self: *Thread, now: bool) void {
  463      // If we're invisible, we do not draw.
  464      if (!self.flags.visible) return;
  465  
  466      // If the renderer is managing a vsync on its own, we only draw
  467      // when we're forced to via `now`.
  468      if (!now and self.renderer.hasVsync()) return;
  469  
  470      self.renderer.drawFrame(false) catch |err|
  471          log.warn("error drawing err={}", .{err});
  472  }
  473  
  474  fn wakeupCallback(
  475      self_: ?*Thread,
  476      _: *xev.Loop,
  477      _: *xev.Completion,
  478      r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
  479  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  480      _ = r catch |err| {
  481          log.err("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
  482          return .rearm;
  483      };
  484  
  485      const t = self_.?;
  486  
  487      // When we wake up, we check the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
  488      // wake up our thread after publishing.
  489      t.drainMailbox() catch |err|
  490          log.err("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
  491  
  492      // Render immediately
  493      _ = renderCallback(t, undefined, undefined, {});
  494  
  495      // PageList mutations maintain their own compression dirty state. Checking
  496      // it here covers output, resize, and viewport scrolling uniformly.
  497      t.compression.wake(t);
  498  
  499      // The below is not used anymore but if we ever want to introduce
  500      // a configuration to introduce a delay to coalesce renders, we can
  501      // use this.
  502      //
  503      // // If the timer is already active then we don't have to do anything.
  504      // if (t.render_c.state() == .active) return .rearm;
  505      //
  506      // // Timer is not active, let's start it
  507      // t.render_h.run(
  508      //     &t.loop,
  509      //     &t.render_c,
  510      //     10,
  511      //     Thread,
  512      //     t,
  513      //     renderCallback,
  514      // );
  515  
  516      return .rearm;
  517  }
  518  
  519  fn drawNowCallback(
  520      self_: ?*Thread,
  521      _: *xev.Loop,
  522      _: *xev.Completion,
  523      r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
  524  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  525      _ = r catch |err| {
  526          log.err("error in draw now err={}", .{err});
  527          return .rearm;
  528      };
  529  
  530      // Draw immediately
  531      const t = self_.?;
  532      t.drawFrame(true);
  533  
  534      return .rearm;
  535  }
  536  
  537  fn renderCallback(
  538      self_: ?*Thread,
  539      _: *xev.Loop,
  540      _: *xev.Completion,
  541      r: xev.Timer.RunError!void,
  542  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  543      _ = r catch |err| switch (err) {
  544          // Sent when a scheduled animation wakeup is superseded by a
  545          // newer one (Timer.reset cancels the pending run). Nothing to
  546          // do; the replacement timer carries on.
  547          error.Canceled => return .disarm,
  548          else => unreachable,
  549      };
  550      const t: *Thread = self_ orelse {
  551          // This shouldn't happen so we log it.
  552          log.warn("render callback fired without data set", .{});
  553          return .disarm;
  554      };
  555  
  556      // If the display is now unrealized, release GPU resources now
  557      // we're on the render thread, and do not try to update and draw
  558      // this frame.
  559      if (!t.renderer.display_realized) {
  560          t.renderer.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  561          defer t.renderer.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
  562  
  563          t.renderer.releaseGpuResources();
  564          return .disarm;
  565      }
  566  
  567      // If we're not visible there's no point spending CPU rebuilding cells —
  568      // we'll catch up when the .visible mailbox message flips us back on.
  569      // Kitty graphics animations pause with us and resume on visibility.
  570      if (!t.flags.visible) return .disarm;
  571  
  572      // Update our frame data
  573      t.renderer.updateFrame(
  574          t.state,
  575          t.flags.cursor_blink_visible,
  576      ) catch |err|
  577          log.warn("error rendering err={}", .{err});
  578  
  579      // Draw
  580      t.drawFrame(false);
  581  
  582      // Schedule the next animation wake, if the renderer needs one.
  583      t.armAnimationTimer();
  584  
  585      return .disarm;
  586  }
  587  
  588  /// Schedule the animation timer for the renderer's next animation
  589  /// wake, if it needs one.
  590  ///
  591  /// This is called after every frame update or animation draw and
  592  /// whenever the wake inputs change (focus, config, visibility
  593  /// regain). Resetting a pending timer is always safe: every call
  594  /// recomputes the wake, so the deadline only ever moves toward the
  595  /// actual next wake.
  596  fn armAnimationTimer(self: *Thread) void {
  597      const wake = self.renderer.animationWake() orelse return;
  598      self.animation_wake = wake.kind;
  599      self.render_h.reset(
  600          &self.loop,
  601          &self.render_c,
  602          &self.render_c_cancel,
  603          wake.delay_ms,
  604          Thread,
  605          self,
  606          animationTimerCallback,
  607      );
  608  }
  609  
  610  fn animationTimerCallback(
  611      self_: ?*Thread,
  612      _: *xev.Loop,
  613      _: *xev.Completion,
  614      r: xev.Timer.RunError!void,
  615  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  616      _ = r catch |err| switch (err) {
  617          // Sent when a scheduled animation wake is superseded by a
  618          // newer one (Timer.reset cancels the pending run). Nothing to
  619          // do; the replacement timer carries on.
  620          error.Canceled => return .disarm,
  621          else => unreachable,
  622      };
  623      const t: *Thread = self_ orelse {
  624          // This shouldn't happen so we log it.
  625          log.warn("animation callback fired without data set", .{});
  626          return .disarm;
  627      };
  628  
  629      // Animations pause entirely while we're invisible; the .visible
  630      // mailbox message re-arms us when we can be seen again.
  631      if (!t.flags.visible) return .disarm;
  632  
  633      switch (t.animation_wake) {
  634          // Frame data must be updated (a Kitty animation frame is
  635          // due). renderCallback updates, draws, and re-arms us.
  636          .update => return renderCallback(
  637              t,
  638              undefined,
  639              undefined,
  640              {},
  641          ),
  642  
  643          // A redraw alone suffices (custom shader time uniform).
  644          // Draw calls don't update from the terminal state so they
  645          // are much cheaper than a frame update.
  646          .draw => {
  647              t.drawFrame(false);
  648              t.armAnimationTimer();
  649              return .disarm;
  650          },
  651      }
  652  }
  653  
  654  fn cursorTimerCallback(
  655      self_: ?*Thread,
  656      _: *xev.Loop,
  657      _: *xev.Completion,
  658      r: xev.Timer.RunError!void,
  659  ) xev.CallbackAction {
  660      _ = r catch |err| switch (err) {
  661          // This is sent when our timer is canceled. That's fine.
  662          error.Canceled => return .disarm,
  663  
  664          else => {
  665              log.warn("error in cursor timer callback err={}", .{err});
  666              unreachable;
  667          },
  668      };
  669  
  670      const t: *Thread = self_ orelse {
  671          // This shouldn't happen so we log it.
  672          log.warn("render callback fired without data set", .{});
  673          return .disarm;
  674      };
  675  
  676      t.flags.cursor_blink_visible = !t.flags.cursor_blink_visible;
  677      t.wakeup.notify() catch {};
  678  
  679      t.cursor_h.run(
  680          &t.loop,
  681          &t.cursor_c,
  682          cursorBlinkInterval(),
  683          Thread,
  684          t,
  685          cursorTimerCallback,
  686      );
  687      return .disarm;
  688  }

n36 src/renderer/generic.zig:1136-1190
 1136          pub fn animationWake(self: *const Self) ?AnimationWake {
 1137              // Custom shaders animate by redrawing on a fixed cadence,
 1138              // gated by configuration and focus.
 1139              const shader_delay: ?u64 = shader: {
 1140                  if (!self.has_custom_shaders) break :shader null;
 1141                  break :shader switch (self.config.custom_shader_animation) {
 1142                      .false => null,
 1143                      .always => draw_interval_ms,
 1144                      .true => if (self.focused) draw_interval_ms else null,
 1145                  };
 1146              };
 1147  
 1148              // Kitty animations tick during updateFrame; between
 1149              // updates the deadline is absolute on the animation
 1150              // clock, so a stream of draw wakes recomputing this
 1151              // cannot starve it into the future.
 1152              const kitty_delay: ?u64 = kitty: {
 1153                  const next = self.kitty_animation_next_ms orelse break :kitty null;
 1154                  const base = self.kitty_animation_clock orelse break :kitty null;
 1155                  const now: std.Io.Timestamp = .now(global.io(), .awake);
 1156                  const now_ms: u64 = @intCast(@divTrunc(
 1157                      base.durationTo(now).nanoseconds,
 1158                      std.time.ns_per_ms,
 1159                  ));
 1160                  // Never wake faster than the draw interval; an
 1161                  // overdue frame is picked up on the next wake.
 1162                  break :kitty @max(next -| now_ms, draw_interval_ms);
 1163              };
 1164  
 1165              // An update wake includes a draw, so it wins ties.
 1166              if (kitty_delay) |k| {
 1167                  if (shader_delay == null or k <= shader_delay.?) {
 1168                      return .{ .delay_ms = k, .kind = .update };
 1169                  }
 1170              }
 1171  
 1172              if (shader_delay) |s| return .{ .delay_ms = s, .kind = .draw };
 1173  
 1174              return null;
 1175          }
 1176  
 1177          /// Returns true if the display link is running, meaning frames
 1178          /// are paced by display refresh. In that case the render thread
 1179          /// must only draw when the display link wakes it through
 1180          /// `draw_now`, and not on its own.
 1181          ///
 1182          /// This reflects the link's actual state. Right after
 1183          /// `syncDisplayLink` asks the link to start, this is still false
 1184          /// until the main thread starts it, and the render thread draws
 1185          /// immediately in the meantime.
 1186          pub fn hasVsync(self: *const Self) bool {
 1187              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return false;
 1188              const display_link = self.display_link orelse return false;
 1189              return display_link.isRunning();
 1190          }
n36 src/renderer/generic.zig:1273-1312
 1273          fn syncDisplayLink(
 1274              self: *Self,
 1275              display_id: ?u32,
 1276              draw_now: ?*xev.Async,
 1277          ) void {
 1278              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
 1279  
 1280              const display_link = self.display_link orelse display_link: {
 1281                  if (!self.config.vsync) return;
 1282                  const callback = draw_now orelse return;
 1283                  const result = DisplayLink.create(
 1284                      self.alloc,
 1285                      xev.Async,
 1286                      &displayLinkCallback,
 1287                      callback,
 1288                  ) catch |err| {
 1289                      // A locked macOS session can temporarily have no active
 1290                      // displays. Rendering continues without vsync and the
 1291                      // next call to this function tries again.
 1292                      log.warn("error creating display link; using fallback rendering err={}", .{err});
 1293                      return;
 1294                  };
 1295  
 1296                  self.display_link = result;
 1297                  log.info("created display link", .{});
 1298                  break :display_link result;
 1299              };
 1300  
 1301              if (display_id) |id| display_link.setDisplay(id);
 1302  
 1303              const should_run =
 1304                  // Non-visible windows never vsync
 1305                  self.visible and
 1306                  // Only vsync if we have cell or image changes or animation
 1307                  (self.cells_rebuilt or
 1308                      self.images_rebuilt or
 1309                      self.animationWake() != null);
 1310  
 1311              display_link.setRunning(should_run);
 1312          }

n36 src/renderer/generic.zig:1716-1731
 1716          pub fn drawFrame(
 1717              self: *Self,
 1718              sync: bool,
 1719          ) !void {
 1720              // Everything that touches draw state happens under the draw
 1721              // mutex. The display link is synced after the mutex is
 1722              // released. It doesn't need the lock, and this keeps the
 1723              // critical section to just drawing.

 1725                  self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1726                  defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());

 1728              };
 1729

 1731          }

n37 src/renderer/metal/IOSurfaceLayer.zig:53-123
   53  pub inline fn setSurface(self: *IOSurfaceLayer, surface: *IOSurface) !void {
   54      // We retain the surface to make sure it's not GC'd
   55      // before we can set it as the contents of the layer.
   56      //
   57      // We release in the callback after setting the contents.
   58      surface.retain();
   59      // NOTE: Since `self.layer` is passed as an `objc.c.id`, it's
   60      //       automatically retained when the block is copied, so we
   61      //       don't need to retain it ourselves like with the surface.
   62  
   63      var block = SetSurfaceBlock.init(.{
   64          .layer = self.layer.value,
   65          .surface = surface,
   66      }, &setSurfaceCallback);
   67  
   68      // We check if we're on the main thread and run the block directly if so.
   69      const NSThread = objc.getClass("NSThread").?;
   70      if (NSThread.msgSend(bool, "isMainThread", .{})) {
   71          setSurfaceCallback(&block);
   72      } else {
   73          // NOTE: The block will be copied when we pass it to dispatch_async,
   74          //       and then automatically be deallocated by the objc runtime
   75          //       once it's executed.
   76  
   77          macos.dispatch.dispatch_async(
   78              @ptrCast(macos.dispatch.queue.getMain()),
   79              @ptrCast(&block),
   80          );
   81      }
   82  }
   83  
   84  /// Sets the layer's `contents` to the provided IOSurface.
   85  ///
   86  /// Does not ensure this happens on the main thread.
   87  pub inline fn setSurfaceSync(self: *IOSurfaceLayer, surface: *IOSurface) void {
   88      self.layer.setProperty("contents", surface);
   89  }
   90  
   91  const SetSurfaceBlock = objc.Block(struct {
   92      layer: objc.c.id,
   93      surface: *IOSurface,
   94  }, .{}, void);
   95  
   96  fn setSurfaceCallback(
   97      block: *const SetSurfaceBlock.Context,
   98  ) callconv(.c) void {
   99      const layer = objc.Object.fromId(block.layer);
  100      const surface: *IOSurface = block.surface;
  101  
  102      // See explanation of why we retain and release in `setSurface`.
  103      defer surface.release();
  104  
  105      // We check to see if the surface is the appropriate size for
  106      // the layer, if it's not then we discard it. This is because
  107      // asynchronously drawn frames can sometimes finish just after
  108      // a synchronously drawn frame during a resize, and if we don't
  109      // discard the improperly sized surface it creates jank.
  110      const bounds = layer.getProperty(macos.graphics.Rect, "bounds");
  111      const scale = layer.getProperty(f64, "contentsScale");
  112      const width: usize = @intFromFloat(bounds.size.width * scale);
  113      const height: usize = @intFromFloat(bounds.size.height * scale);
  114      if (width != surface.getWidth() or height != surface.getHeight()) {
  115          log.debug(
  116              "setSurfaceCallback(): surface is wrong size for layer, discarding. surface = {d}x{d}, layer = {d}x{d}",
  117              .{ surface.getWidth(), surface.getHeight(), width, height },
  118          );
  119          return;
  120      }
  121  
  122      layer.setProperty("contents", surface);
  123  }
