n43 macos/Sources/Ghostty/Ghostty.Surface.swift:12-45
   12      @MainActor
   13      final class Surface {
   14          /// Nonisolated because `deinit` is nonisolated and must read this to free the surface.
   15          nonisolated(unsafe) private let surface: ghostty_surface_t
   16  
   17          /// Read the underlying C value for this surface. This is unsafe because the value will be
   18          /// freed when the Surface class is deinitialized.
   19          var unsafeCValue: ghostty_surface_t {
   20              surface
   21          }
   22  
   23          /// Initialize from the C structure.
   24          init(cSurface: ghostty_surface_t) {
   25              self.surface = cSurface
   26          }
   27  
   28          deinit {
   29              guard !Thread.isMainThread else {
   30                  // The surface remains registered with the app and holds unretained
   31                  // userdata until it is freed. When already on the main thread, free
   32                  // it synchronously so teardown completes before we disappear.
   33                  ghostty_surface_free(surface)
   34                  return
   35              }
   36              // deinit is not guaranteed to happen on the main actor and our API
   37              // calls into libghostty must happen there so we capture the surface
   38              // value so we don't capture `self` and then we detach it in a task.
   39              // We can't wait for the task to succeed so this will happen sometime
   40              // but that's okay.
   41              nonisolated(unsafe) let surface = self.surface
   42              Task.detached { @MainActor in
   43                  ghostty_surface_free(surface)
   44              }
   45          }

  210  const Search = struct {
  211      state: terminal.search.Thread,
  212      thread: std.Thread,
  213  
  214      pub fn deinit(self: *Search) void {
  215          // Notify the thread to stop
  216          self.state.stop.notify() catch |err| log.err(
  217              "error notifying search thread to stop, may stall err={}",
  218              .{err},
  219          );
  220  
  221          // Wait for the OS thread to quit
  222          self.thread.join();
  223  
  224          // Now it is safe to deinit the state
  225          self.state.deinit();
  226      }

n44 src/Surface.zig:829-879
  829  pub fn deinit(self: *Surface) void {
  830      // Stop search thread
  831      if (self.search) |*s| s.deinit();
  832  
  833      // Stop rendering thread
  834      {
  835          self.renderer_thread.stop.notify() catch |err|
  836              log.err("error notifying renderer thread to stop, may stall err={}", .{err});
  837          self.renderer_thr.join();
  838      }
  839  
  840      // Stop our IO thread
  841      {
  842          self.io_thread.stop.notify() catch |err|
  843              log.err("error notifying io thread to stop, may stall err={}", .{err});
  844          self.io_thr.join();
  845      }
  846  
  847      // We need to deinit AFTER everything is stopped, since there are
  848      // shared values between the two threads.
  849      //
  850      // The renderer must be deinitialized before the renderer thread:
  851      // on macOS the renderer's display link callback points at the
  852      // thread's `draw_now` handle, and the renderer's deinit is what
  853      // stops the display link for good.
  854      self.renderer.deinit();
  855      self.renderer_thread.deinit();
  856      self.io_thread.deinit();
  857      self.mouse.selection_gesture.deinit(&self.io.terminal);
  858      self.io.deinit();
  859  
  860      if (self.inspector) |v| {
  861          v.deinit(self.alloc);
  862          self.alloc.destroy(v);
  863      }
  864  
  865      // Clean up our keyboard state
  866      for (self.keyboard.sequence_queued.items) |req| req.deinit();
  867      self.keyboard.sequence_queued.deinit(self.alloc);
  868      self.keyboard.table_stack.deinit(self.alloc);
  869  
  870      // Clean up our font grid
  871      self.app.font_grid_set.deref(self.font_grid_key);
  872  
  873      // Clean up our render state
  874      if (self.renderer_state.preedit) |p| self.alloc.free(p.codepoints);
  875      self.alloc.destroy(self.renderer_state.mutex);
  876      self.config.deinit();
  877  
  878      log.info("surface closed id={x}", .{self.id});
  879  }

  118  pub fn deinit(self: *Thread) void {

  120      self.wakeup.deinit();

  122      self.loop.deinit();

n44 src/terminal/search/Thread.zig:160-192
  160      // Start the async handlers
  161      self.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, Thread, self, wakeupCallback);
  162      self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, Thread, self, stopCallback);
  163  
  164      // Send an initial wakeup so we drain our mailbox immediately.
  165      try self.wakeup.notify();
  166  
  167      // Start the refresh timer
  168      self.startRefreshTimer();
  169  
  170      // Run
  171      log.debug("starting search thread", .{});
  172      defer {
  173          log.debug("starting search thread shutdown", .{});
  174  
  175          // Send the quit message
  176          if (self.opts.event_cb) |cb| {
  177              cb(.quit, self.opts.event_userdata);
  178          }
  179      }
  180  
  181      // Unlike some of our other threads, we interleave search work
  182      // with our xev loop so that we can try to make forward search progress
  183      // while also listening for messages.
  184      while (true) {
  185          // If our loop is canceled then we drain our messages and quit.
  186          if (self.loop.stopped()) {
  187              while (self.mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
  188                  log.debug("mailbox message ignored during shutdown={}", .{message});
  189              }
  190  
  191              return;
  192          }

n44 src/renderer/generic.zig:841-874
  841          pub fn deinit(self: *Self) void {
  842              // This only deinitializes and frees CPU-side state
  843              // and does not free GPU resources like the swap chain and
  844              // shaders. Those are freed with `releaseGpuResources`.
  845  
  846              if (self.overlay) |*overlay| overlay.deinit(self.alloc);
  847              self.terminal_state.deinit(self.alloc);
  848              if (self.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  849              if (self.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  850  
  851              self.latest_frame.deinit(global.io());
  852  
  853              // Stop and release the display link. Surfaces are destroyed
  854              // on the main thread, where `close` stops the link before
  855              // returning. After this the display link callback is never
  856              // invoked again, so `renderer.Thread` can free the `draw_now`
  857              // handle the callback points at.
  858              if (DisplayLink != void) {
  859                  if (self.display_link) |display_link| {
  860                      display_link.close();
  861                      self.display_link = null;
  862                  }
  863              }
  864  
  865              self.cells.deinit(self.alloc);
  866  
  867              self.font_shaper.deinit();
  868              self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
  869  
  870              self.config.deinit();
  871              self.api.deinit();
  872  
  873              self.* = undefined;
  874          }
n44 src/renderer/generic.zig:911-974
  911          /// Callback called by renderer.Thread when it exits. Called on the
  912          /// render thread. Releases all GPU resources before the API tears down
  913          /// its context, since after this the GL context will be gone.
  914          pub fn threadExit(self: *Self) void {
  915              {
  916                  self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  917                  defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
  918  
  919                  // Release swap chain and shaders. Shaders are only freed
  920                  // while unrealized and not every apprt unrealizes before
  921                  // destroying a surface (macOS never does), so mark it
  922                  // unrealized. This also stops any later draw.
  923                  self.display_realized = false;
  924                  self.releaseGpuResources();
  925  
  926                  // We don't release images in `releaseGpuResources`
  927                  // since it can be called whenever the terminal is
  928                  // occluded or unrealized, and we don't want to
  929                  // reupload images every time that happens.
  930                  self.images.deinit(self.alloc);
  931                  self.images = .empty;
  932  
  933                  if (self.bg_image) |img| {
  934                      img.deinit(self.alloc);
  935                      self.bg_image = null;
  936                  }
  937              }
  938  
  939              // If our API has to do things on thread exit, let it.
  940              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "threadExit")) {
  941                  self.api.threadExit();
  942              }
  943          }
  944  
  945          /// Called by renderer.Thread when it starts the main loop.
  946          pub fn loopEnter(self: *Self, thr: *renderer.Thread) !void {
  947              // If our API has to do things on loop enter, let it.
  948              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopEnter")) {
  949                  self.api.loopEnter();
  950              }
  951  
  952              // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
  953              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
  954  
  955              self.syncDisplayLink(null, &thr.draw_now);
  956          }
  957  
  958          /// Called by renderer.Thread when it exits the main loop.
  959          pub fn loopExit(self: *Self) void {
  960              // If our API has to do things on loop exit, let it.
  961              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopExit")) {
  962                  self.api.loopExit();
  963              }
  964  
  965              // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
  966              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
  967  
  968              // Ask the display link to stop. The stop itself happens on
  969              // the main thread shortly after. `deinit` later stops the
  970              // link for certain before anything the display link callback
  971              // references is freed.
  972              const display_link = self.display_link orelse return;
  973              display_link.setRunning(false);
  974          }

 1244          pub fn releaseGpuResources(self: *Self) void {
 1245              if (self.swap_chain) |*sc| {
 1246                  // Waits for any in-flight frames to complete, then
 1247                  // frees all GPU resources.
 1248                  sc.deinit();
 1249                  self.swap_chain = null;
 1250              }
 1251  
 1252              // Release the shaders as well if we're unrealized.
 1253              if (!self.display_realized) {
 1254                  self.shaders.deinit(self.alloc);
 1255              }
 1256          }
n44 src/renderer/generic.zig:1273-1298
 1273          fn syncDisplayLink(
 1274              self: *Self,
 1275              display_id: ?u32,
 1276              draw_now: ?*xev.Async,
 1277          ) void {
 1278              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
 1279  
 1280              const display_link = self.display_link orelse display_link: {
 1281                  if (!self.config.vsync) return;
 1282                  const callback = draw_now orelse return;
 1283                  const result = DisplayLink.create(
 1284                      self.alloc,
 1285                      xev.Async,
 1286                      &displayLinkCallback,
 1287                      callback,
 1288                  ) catch |err| {
 1289                      // A locked macOS session can temporarily have no active
 1290                      // displays. Rendering continues without vsync and the
 1291                      // next call to this function tries again.
 1292                      log.warn("error creating display link; using fallback rendering err={}", .{err});
 1293                      return;
 1294                  };
 1295  
 1296                  self.display_link = result;
 1297                  log.info("created display link", .{});
 1298                  break :display_link result;
n44 src/renderer/DisplayLink.zig:120-146
  120  /// thread.
  121  ///
  122  /// The link is stopped before this returns, so the output callback is
  123  /// not invoked again and whatever `userdata` pointed at can be freed.
  124  ///
  125  /// No other method may be called on this link after or concurrently
  126  /// with `close`. In practice the render thread that makes those calls
  127  /// has already exited by the time the renderer is deinitialized.
  128  pub fn close(self: *DisplayLink) void {
  129      assert(!self.closed);
  130      self.closed = true;
  131  
  132      if (self.link.isRunning()) self.link.stop() catch |err| {
  133          log.warn("error stopping display link err={}", .{err});
  134      };
  135      self.link.release();
  136  
  137      // If a sync is queued on the main thread it runs after we return
  138      // and still needs this struct, so it frees it instead of us.
  139      if (!self.sync_queued.load(.seq_cst)) self.destroy();
  140  }
  141  
  142  fn destroy(self: *DisplayLink) void {
  143      const alloc = self.alloc;
  144      self.* = undefined;
  145      alloc.destroy(self);
  146  }
n44 src/renderer/DisplayLink.zig:205-219
  205  fn syncCallback(ud: ?*anyopaque) callconv(.c) void {
  206      const self: *DisplayLink = @ptrCast(@alignCast(ud.?));
  207  
  208      // Clear this before reading the requested state. See queueSync.
  209      self.sync_queued.store(false, .seq_cst);
  210  
  211      // If the owner closed the link while we were queued, it left the
  212      // struct for us to free. See close.
  213      if (self.closed) {
  214          self.destroy();
  215          return;
  216      }
  217  
  218      self.sync();
  219  }

n44 src/termio/Thread.zig:259-279
  259      // This is the data sent to xev callbacks. We want a pointer to both
  260      // ourselves and the thread data so we can thread that through (pun intended).
  261      var cb: CallbackData = .{ .self = self, .io = io };
  262  
  263      // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This allows the implementation
  264      // to hook into the event loop as needed. The thread data is created
  265      // on the stack here so that it has a stable pointer throughout the
  266      // lifetime of the thread.
  267      try io.threadEnter(self, &cb.data);
  268      defer cb.data.deinit();
  269      defer io.threadExit(&cb.data);
  270  
  271      // Start the async handlers.
  272      mailbox.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, CallbackData, &cb, wakeupCallback);
  273      self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, CallbackData, &cb, stopCallback);
  274  
  275      // Run
  276      log.debug("starting IO thread", .{});
  277      defer log.debug("starting IO thread shutdown", .{});
  278      try self.loop.run(.until_done);
  279  }

n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:196-230
  196  pub fn threadExit(self: *Exec, td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData) void {
  197      assert(td.backend == .exec);
  198      const exec = &td.backend.exec;
  199  
  200      if (exec.exited) self.subprocess.externalExit();
  201      self.subprocess.stop();
  202  
  203      // Quit our read thread after exiting the subprocess so that
  204      // we don't get stuck waiting for data to stop flowing if it is
  205      // a particularly noisy process.
  206      switch (posix.errno(posix.system.write(exec.read_thread_pipe, "x", 1))) {
  207          .SUCCESS => {},
  208  
  209          // EPIPE means that our read thread is closed already, which is
  210          // completely fine since that is what we were trying to achieve.
  211          .PIPE => {},
  212  
  213          else => |e| log.warn(
  214              "error writing to read thread quit pipe err=E{s}",
  215              .{@tagName(e)},
  216          ),
  217      }
  218  
  219      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
  220          // Interrupt the blocking read so the thread can see the quit message
  221          if (windows.exp.kernel32.CancelIoEx(exec.read_thread_fd, null) == windows.FALSE) {
  222              switch (windows.GetLastError()) {
  223                  .NOT_FOUND => {},
  224                  else => |err| log.warn("error interrupting read thread err={}", .{err}),
  225              }
  226          }
  227      }
  228  
  229      exec.read_thread.join();
  230  }
n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:549-565
  549      pub fn deinit(self: *ThreadData, alloc: Allocator) void {
  550          _ = posix.system.close(self.read_thread_pipe);
  551  
  552          // Clear our write pool. We know we aren't ever going to do
  553          // any more IO since we stop our data stream below so we can just
  554          // drop this.
  555          self.write_pool.deinit(alloc);
  556  
  557          // Stop our process watcher
  558          if (self.process) |*p| p.deinit();
  559  
  560          // Stop our write stream
  561          self.write_stream.deinit();
  562  
  563          // Stop our termios timer
  564          self.termios_timer.deinit();
  565      }

n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:1107-1133
 1107      pub fn externalExit(self: *Subprocess) void {
 1108          self.process = null;
 1109      }
 1110  
 1111      /// Stop the subprocess. This is safe to call anytime. This will wait
 1112      /// for the subprocess to register that it has been signalled, but not
 1113      /// for it to terminate, so it will not block.
 1114      /// This does not close the pty.
 1115      pub fn stop(self: *Subprocess) void {
 1116          switch (self.process orelse return) {
 1117              .fork_exec => |*cmd| {
 1118                  // Note: this will also wait for the command to exit, so
 1119                  // DO NOT call cmd.wait
 1120                  killCommand(cmd) catch |err|
 1121                      log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
 1122              },
 1123  
 1124              .flatpak => |*cmd| if (comptime build_config.flatpak) {
 1125                  killCommandFlatpak(cmd) catch |err|
 1126                      log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
 1127                  _ = cmd.wait() catch |err|
 1128                      log.err("error waiting for command to exit: {}", .{err});
 1129              },
 1130          }
 1131  
 1132          self.process = null;
 1133      }
n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:1158-1258
 1158      /// Kill the underlying subprocess. This sends a SIGHUP to the child
 1159      /// process. This also waits for the command to exit and will return the
 1160      /// exit code.
 1161      fn killCommand(command: *Command) !void {
 1162          if (command.pid) |pid| {
 1163              switch (builtin.target.os.tag) {
 1164                  .windows => {
 1165                      if (windows.exp.kernel32.TerminateProcess(pid, 0) == windows.FALSE) {
 1166                          return windows.unexpectedError(windows.GetLastError());
 1167                      }
 1168  
 1169                      _ = try command.wait(false);
 1170                  },
 1171  
 1172                  else => try killPid(pid),
 1173              }
 1174          }
 1175      }
 1176  
 1177      fn killPid(pid: c.pid_t) !void {
 1178          const pgid = getpgid(pid) orelse return;
 1179  
 1180          // It is possible to send a killpg between the time that
 1181          // our child process calls setsid but before or simultaneous
 1182          // to calling execve. In this case, the direct child dies
 1183          // but grandchildren survive. To work around this, we loop
 1184          // and repeatedly kill the process group until all
 1185          // descendents are well and truly dead. We will not rest
 1186          // until the entire family tree is obliterated.
 1187          while (true) {
 1188              switch (posix.errno(c.killpg(pgid, c.SIGHUP))) {
 1189                  .SUCCESS => log.debug("process group killed pgid={}", .{pgid}),
 1190                  else => |err| killpg: {
 1191                      if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) and
 1192                          err == .PERM)
 1193                      {
 1194                          log.debug("killpg failed with EPERM, expected on Darwin and ignoring", .{});
 1195                          break :killpg;
 1196                      }
 1197  
 1198                      log.warn("error killing process group pgid={} err={}", .{ pgid, err });
 1199                      return error.KillFailed;
 1200                  },
 1201              }
 1202  
 1203              // See Command.zig wait for why we specify WNOHANG.
 1204              // The gist is that it lets us detect when children
 1205              // are still alive without blocking so that we can
 1206              // kill them again.
 1207              const res_pid = while (true) {
 1208                  const rc = posix.system.waitpid(pid, null, std.c.W.NOHANG);
 1209                  if (posix.errno(rc) == .INTR) continue;
 1210                  break rc;
 1211              };
 1212              log.debug("waitpid result={}", .{res_pid});
 1213              if (res_pid != 0) break;
 1214              try std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake);
 1215          }
 1216      }
 1217  
 1218      fn getpgid(pid: c.pid_t) ?c.pid_t {
 1219          // Get our process group ID. Before the child pid calls setsid
 1220          // the pgid will be ours because we forked it. Its possible that
 1221          // we may be calling this before setsid if we are killing a surface
 1222          // VERY quickly after starting it.
 1223          const my_pgid = c.getpgid(0);
 1224  
 1225          // We loop while pgid == my_pgid. The expectation if we have a valid
 1226          // pid is that setsid will eventually be called because it is the
 1227          // FIRST thing the child process does and as far as I can tell,
 1228          // setsid cannot fail. I'm sure that's not true, but I'd rather
 1229          // have a bug reported than defensively program against it now.
 1230          while (true) {
 1231              const pgid = c.getpgid(pid);
 1232              if (pgid == my_pgid) {
 1233                  log.warn("pgid is our own, retrying", .{});
 1234                  std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake) catch {};
 1235                  continue;
 1236              }
 1237  
 1238              // Don't know why it would be zero but its not a valid pid
 1239              if (pgid == 0) return null;
 1240  
 1241              // If the pid doesn't exist then... we're done!
 1242              if (pgid == c.ESRCH) return null;
 1243  
 1244              // If we have an error we're done.
 1245              if (pgid < 0) {
 1246                  log.warn("error getting pgid for kill", .{});
 1247                  return null;
 1248              }
 1249  
 1250              return pgid;
 1251          }
 1252      }
 1253  
 1254      /// Kill the underlying process started via Flatpak host command.
 1255      /// This sends a signal via the Flatpak API.
 1256      fn killCommandFlatpak(command: *FlatpakHostCommand) !void {
 1257          try command.signal(c.SIGHUP, true);
 1258      }
n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:1462-1492
 1462          const gather_thread = std.Thread.spawn(
 1463              .{},
 1464              gatherMainPosix,
 1465              .{ fd, quit, &pipeline },
 1466          ) catch |err| {
 1467              // If we can't spawn a thread the process is already
 1468              // doomed (every surface spawns several), so don't try
 1469              // to limp along.
 1470              log.err("read thread exiting, failed to spawn gather thread err={}", .{err});
 1471              return;
 1472          };
 1473          defer gather_thread.join();
 1474          if (comptime !builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
 1475              gather_thread.setName(global.io(), "io-gather") catch {};
 1476          }
 1477  
 1478          // This thread is the parse stage. We consume batches in ring
 1479          // order until the gather stage reports the stream is over and
 1480          // the ring is drained.
 1481          while (true) {
 1482              const batch: []const u8 = batch: {
 1483                  pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1484                  defer pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1485                  while (pipeline.count == 0) {
 1486                      if (pipeline.done) return;
 1487                      pipeline.batch_ready.waitUncancelable(global.io(), &pipeline.mutex);
 1488                  }
 1489                  const slot = pipeline.tail;
 1490                  break :batch pipeline.bufs[slot][0..pipeline.lens[slot]];
 1491              };
 1492  
n44 src/termio/Exec.zig:1612-1642
 1612                          // the idle wake so it can interrupt our poll
 1613                          // the moment that changes.
 1614                          {
 1615                              pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1616                              defer pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1617                              if (pipeline.count == 0) break :gather;
 1618                              pipeline.bridging = true;
 1619                          }
 1620  
 1621                          const r = posix.poll(
 1622                              &pollfds,
 1623                              bridge_poll_timeout_ms,
 1624                          ) catch |poll_err| {

 1627                              break :gather;
 1628                          };
 1629                          clearBridging(pipeline);
 1630  
 1631                          // Quiet for a full timeout means the burst
 1632                          // ended.
 1633                          if (r == 0) break :gather;
 1634  
 1635                          // On a quit signal we deliver what we have
 1636                          // and stop.
 1637                          if (pollfds[1].revents & posix.POLL.IN != 0) {
 1638                              log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
 1639                              fatal = true;
 1640                              break :gather;
 1641                          }

 1714              // stage only writes to it while we're bridging.

 1716                  log.warn("poll failed on read thread, exiting early err={}", .{err});
 1717                  return;
 1718              };

 1727              // read thread.

 1729                  log.info("pty fd closed, read thread exiting", .{});
 1730                  return;
 1731              }
 1732          }

n44 src/font/SharedGridSet.zig:411-431
  411  /// Decrement the ref count for the given key. If the ref count is zero,
  412  /// the grid will be deinitialized and removed from the map.j:w
  413  pub fn deref(self: *SharedGridSet, key: Key) void {
  414      self.lock.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  415      defer self.lock.unlock(global.io());
  416  
  417      const entry = self.map.getEntry(key) orelse return;
  418      assert(entry.value_ptr.ref >= 1);
  419  
  420      // If we have more than one reference, decrement and return.
  421      if (entry.value_ptr.ref > 1) {
  422          entry.value_ptr.ref -= 1;
  423          return;
  424      }
  425  
  426      // We are at a zero ref count so deinit the group and remove.
  427      entry.key_ptr.deinit();
  428      entry.value_ptr.grid.deinit(self.alloc);
  429      self.alloc.destroy(entry.value_ptr.grid);
  430      self.map.removeByPtr(entry.key_ptr);
  431  }

  210  const Search = struct {
  211      state: terminal.search.Thread,
  212      thread: std.Thread,
  213  
  214      pub fn deinit(self: *Search) void {
  215          // Notify the thread to stop
  216          self.state.stop.notify() catch |err| log.err(
  217              "error notifying search thread to stop, may stall err={}",
  218              .{err},
  219          );

  223  
  224          // Now it is safe to deinit the state
  225          self.state.deinit();
  226      }

n45 src/termio/Thread.zig:259-279
  259      // This is the data sent to xev callbacks. We want a pointer to both
  260      // ourselves and the thread data so we can thread that through (pun intended).
  261      var cb: CallbackData = .{ .self = self, .io = io };
  262  
  263      // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This allows the implementation
  264      // to hook into the event loop as needed. The thread data is created
  265      // on the stack here so that it has a stable pointer throughout the
  266      // lifetime of the thread.
  267      try io.threadEnter(self, &cb.data);
  268      defer cb.data.deinit();
  269      defer io.threadExit(&cb.data);
  270  
  271      // Start the async handlers.
  272      mailbox.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, CallbackData, &cb, wakeupCallback);
  273      self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, CallbackData, &cb, stopCallback);
  274  
  275      // Run
  276      log.debug("starting IO thread", .{});
  277      defer log.debug("starting IO thread shutdown", .{});
  278      try self.loop.run(.until_done);
  279  }

n46 src/termio/Thread.zig:259-279
  259      // This is the data sent to xev callbacks. We want a pointer to both
  260      // ourselves and the thread data so we can thread that through (pun intended).
  261      var cb: CallbackData = .{ .self = self, .io = io };
  262  
  263      // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This allows the implementation
  264      // to hook into the event loop as needed. The thread data is created
  265      // on the stack here so that it has a stable pointer throughout the
  266      // lifetime of the thread.
  267      try io.threadEnter(self, &cb.data);
  268      defer cb.data.deinit();
  269      defer io.threadExit(&cb.data);
  270  
  271      // Start the async handlers.
  272      mailbox.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, CallbackData, &cb, wakeupCallback);
  273      self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, CallbackData, &cb, stopCallback);
  274  
  275      // Run
  276      log.debug("starting IO thread", .{});
  277      defer log.debug("starting IO thread shutdown", .{});
  278      try self.loop.run(.until_done);
  279  }

n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:196-230
  196  pub fn threadExit(self: *Exec, td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData) void {
  197      assert(td.backend == .exec);
  198      const exec = &td.backend.exec;
  199  
  200      if (exec.exited) self.subprocess.externalExit();
  201      self.subprocess.stop();
  202  
  203      // Quit our read thread after exiting the subprocess so that
  204      // we don't get stuck waiting for data to stop flowing if it is
  205      // a particularly noisy process.
  206      switch (posix.errno(posix.system.write(exec.read_thread_pipe, "x", 1))) {
  207          .SUCCESS => {},
  208  
  209          // EPIPE means that our read thread is closed already, which is
  210          // completely fine since that is what we were trying to achieve.
  211          .PIPE => {},
  212  
  213          else => |e| log.warn(
  214              "error writing to read thread quit pipe err=E{s}",
  215              .{@tagName(e)},
  216          ),
  217      }
  218  
  219      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
  220          // Interrupt the blocking read so the thread can see the quit message
  221          if (windows.exp.kernel32.CancelIoEx(exec.read_thread_fd, null) == windows.FALSE) {
  222              switch (windows.GetLastError()) {
  223                  .NOT_FOUND => {},
  224                  else => |err| log.warn("error interrupting read thread err={}", .{err}),
  225              }
  226          }
  227      }
  228  
  229      exec.read_thread.join();
  230  }
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:549-565
  549      pub fn deinit(self: *ThreadData, alloc: Allocator) void {
  550          _ = posix.system.close(self.read_thread_pipe);
  551  
  552          // Clear our write pool. We know we aren't ever going to do
  553          // any more IO since we stop our data stream below so we can just
  554          // drop this.
  555          self.write_pool.deinit(alloc);
  556  
  557          // Stop our process watcher
  558          if (self.process) |*p| p.deinit();
  559  
  560          // Stop our write stream
  561          self.write_stream.deinit();
  562  
  563          // Stop our termios timer
  564          self.termios_timer.deinit();
  565      }
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1105-1133
 1105      /// Called to notify that we exited externally so we can unset our
 1106      /// running state.
 1107      pub fn externalExit(self: *Subprocess) void {
 1108          self.process = null;
 1109      }
 1110  
 1111      /// Stop the subprocess. This is safe to call anytime. This will wait
 1112      /// for the subprocess to register that it has been signalled, but not
 1113      /// for it to terminate, so it will not block.
 1114      /// This does not close the pty.
 1115      pub fn stop(self: *Subprocess) void {
 1116          switch (self.process orelse return) {
 1117              .fork_exec => |*cmd| {
 1118                  // Note: this will also wait for the command to exit, so
 1119                  // DO NOT call cmd.wait
 1120                  killCommand(cmd) catch |err|
 1121                      log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
 1122              },
 1123  
 1124              .flatpak => |*cmd| if (comptime build_config.flatpak) {
 1125                  killCommandFlatpak(cmd) catch |err|
 1126                      log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
 1127                  _ = cmd.wait() catch |err|
 1128                      log.err("error waiting for command to exit: {}", .{err});
 1129              },
 1130          }
 1131  
 1132          self.process = null;
 1133      }
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1158-1258
 1158      /// Kill the underlying subprocess. This sends a SIGHUP to the child
 1159      /// process. This also waits for the command to exit and will return the
 1160      /// exit code.
 1161      fn killCommand(command: *Command) !void {
 1162          if (command.pid) |pid| {
 1163              switch (builtin.target.os.tag) {
 1164                  .windows => {
 1165                      if (windows.exp.kernel32.TerminateProcess(pid, 0) == windows.FALSE) {
 1166                          return windows.unexpectedError(windows.GetLastError());
 1167                      }
 1168  
 1169                      _ = try command.wait(false);
 1170                  },
 1171  
 1172                  else => try killPid(pid),
 1173              }
 1174          }
 1175      }
 1176  
 1177      fn killPid(pid: c.pid_t) !void {
 1178          const pgid = getpgid(pid) orelse return;
 1179  
 1180          // It is possible to send a killpg between the time that
 1181          // our child process calls setsid but before or simultaneous
 1182          // to calling execve. In this case, the direct child dies
 1183          // but grandchildren survive. To work around this, we loop
 1184          // and repeatedly kill the process group until all
 1185          // descendents are well and truly dead. We will not rest
 1186          // until the entire family tree is obliterated.
 1187          while (true) {
 1188              switch (posix.errno(c.killpg(pgid, c.SIGHUP))) {
 1189                  .SUCCESS => log.debug("process group killed pgid={}", .{pgid}),
 1190                  else => |err| killpg: {
 1191                      if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) and
 1192                          err == .PERM)
 1193                      {
 1194                          log.debug("killpg failed with EPERM, expected on Darwin and ignoring", .{});
 1195                          break :killpg;
 1196                      }
 1197  
 1198                      log.warn("error killing process group pgid={} err={}", .{ pgid, err });
 1199                      return error.KillFailed;
 1200                  },
 1201              }
 1202  
 1203              // See Command.zig wait for why we specify WNOHANG.
 1204              // The gist is that it lets us detect when children
 1205              // are still alive without blocking so that we can
 1206              // kill them again.
 1207              const res_pid = while (true) {
 1208                  const rc = posix.system.waitpid(pid, null, std.c.W.NOHANG);
 1209                  if (posix.errno(rc) == .INTR) continue;
 1210                  break rc;
 1211              };
 1212              log.debug("waitpid result={}", .{res_pid});
 1213              if (res_pid != 0) break;
 1214              try std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake);
 1215          }
 1216      }
 1217  
 1218      fn getpgid(pid: c.pid_t) ?c.pid_t {
 1219          // Get our process group ID. Before the child pid calls setsid
 1220          // the pgid will be ours because we forked it. Its possible that
 1221          // we may be calling this before setsid if we are killing a surface
 1222          // VERY quickly after starting it.
 1223          const my_pgid = c.getpgid(0);
 1224  
 1225          // We loop while pgid == my_pgid. The expectation if we have a valid
 1226          // pid is that setsid will eventually be called because it is the
 1227          // FIRST thing the child process does and as far as I can tell,
 1228          // setsid cannot fail. I'm sure that's not true, but I'd rather
 1229          // have a bug reported than defensively program against it now.
 1230          while (true) {
 1231              const pgid = c.getpgid(pid);
 1232              if (pgid == my_pgid) {
 1233                  log.warn("pgid is our own, retrying", .{});
 1234                  std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake) catch {};
 1235                  continue;
 1236              }
 1237  
 1238              // Don't know why it would be zero but its not a valid pid
 1239              if (pgid == 0) return null;
 1240  
 1241              // If the pid doesn't exist then... we're done!
 1242              if (pgid == c.ESRCH) return null;
 1243  
 1244              // If we have an error we're done.
 1245              if (pgid < 0) {
 1246                  log.warn("error getting pgid for kill", .{});
 1247                  return null;
 1248              }
 1249  
 1250              return pgid;
 1251          }
 1252      }
 1253  
 1254      /// Kill the underlying process started via Flatpak host command.
 1255      /// This sends a signal via the Flatpak API.
 1256      fn killCommandFlatpak(command: *FlatpakHostCommand) !void {
 1257          try command.signal(c.SIGHUP, true);
 1258      }
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1462-1492
 1462          const gather_thread = std.Thread.spawn(
 1463              .{},
 1464              gatherMainPosix,
 1465              .{ fd, quit, &pipeline },
 1466          ) catch |err| {
 1467              // If we can't spawn a thread the process is already
 1468              // doomed (every surface spawns several), so don't try
 1469              // to limp along.
 1470              log.err("read thread exiting, failed to spawn gather thread err={}", .{err});
 1471              return;
 1472          };
 1473          defer gather_thread.join();
 1474          if (comptime !builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
 1475              gather_thread.setName(global.io(), "io-gather") catch {};
 1476          }
 1477  
 1478          // This thread is the parse stage. We consume batches in ring
 1479          // order until the gather stage reports the stream is over and
 1480          // the ring is drained.
 1481          while (true) {
 1482              const batch: []const u8 = batch: {
 1483                  pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1484                  defer pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1485                  while (pipeline.count == 0) {
 1486                      if (pipeline.done) return;
 1487                      pipeline.batch_ready.waitUncancelable(global.io(), &pipeline.mutex);
 1488                  }
 1489                  const slot = pipeline.tail;
 1490                  break :batch pipeline.bufs[slot][0..pipeline.lens[slot]];
 1491              };

n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1526-1549
 1526      fn gatherMainPosix(fd: posix.fd_t, quit: posix.fd_t, pipeline: *Pipeline) void {
 1527          if (builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
 1528              internal_os.macos.pthread_setname_np(&"io-gather".*);
 1529              setQosClass();
 1530          }
 1531  
 1532          // However we exit, tell the parse stage the stream is over so
 1533          // it drains the ring and joins us.
 1534          defer {
 1535              pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1536              pipeline.done = true;
 1537              pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1538              pipeline.batch_ready.signal(global.io());
 1539          }
 1540  
 1541          // The fds we poll: data on the pty, our quit notification,
 1542          // and the parse stage's idle wake. The idle fd only
 1543          // participates in bridge polls (the parse stage only writes
 1544          // while we're bridging), so the outer poll slices it off.
 1545          var pollfds: [3]posix.pollfd = .{
 1546              .{ .fd = fd, .events = posix.POLL.IN, .revents = undefined },
 1547              .{ .fd = quit, .events = posix.POLL.IN, .revents = undefined },
 1548              .{ .fd = pipeline.idle_read_fd, .events = posix.POLL.IN, .revents = undefined },
 1549          };
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1612-1642
 1612                          // the idle wake so it can interrupt our poll
 1613                          // the moment that changes.
 1614                          {
 1615                              pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
 1616                              defer pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
 1617                              if (pipeline.count == 0) break :gather;
 1618                              pipeline.bridging = true;
 1619                          }
 1620  
 1621                          const r = posix.poll(
 1622                              &pollfds,
 1623                              bridge_poll_timeout_ms,
 1624                          ) catch |poll_err| {
 1625                              clearBridging(pipeline);
 1626                              log.warn("bridge poll failed err={}", .{poll_err});
 1627                              break :gather;
 1628                          };
 1629                          clearBridging(pipeline);
 1630  
 1631                          // Quiet for a full timeout means the burst
 1632                          // ended.
 1633                          if (r == 0) break :gather;
 1634  
 1635                          // On a quit signal we deliver what we have
 1636                          // and stop.
 1637                          if (pollfds[1].revents & posix.POLL.IN != 0) {
 1638                              log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
 1639                              fatal = true;
 1640                              break :gather;
 1641                          }
 1642  
n46 src/termio/Exec.zig:1713-1733
 1713              // Wait for data. The idle fd is sliced off: the parse
 1714              // stage only writes to it while we're bridging.
 1715              _ = posix.poll(pollfds[0..2], -1) catch |err| {
 1716                  log.warn("poll failed on read thread, exiting early err={}", .{err});
 1717                  return;
 1718              };
 1719  
 1720              // If our quit fd is set, we're done.
 1721              if (pollfds[1].revents & posix.POLL.IN != 0) {
 1722                  log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
 1723                  return;
 1724              }

 1727              // read thread.
 1728              if (pollfds[0].revents & posix.POLL.HUP != 0) {
 1729                  log.info("pty fd closed, read thread exiting", .{});
 1730                  return;
 1731              }
 1732          }

n47 src/renderer/generic.zig:841-874
  841          pub fn deinit(self: *Self) void {
  842              // This only deinitializes and frees CPU-side state
  843              // and does not free GPU resources like the swap chain and
  844              // shaders. Those are freed with `releaseGpuResources`.
  845  
  846              if (self.overlay) |*overlay| overlay.deinit(self.alloc);
  847              self.terminal_state.deinit(self.alloc);
  848              if (self.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  849              if (self.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
  850  
  851              self.latest_frame.deinit(global.io());
  852  
  853              // Stop and release the display link. Surfaces are destroyed
  854              // on the main thread, where `close` stops the link before
  855              // returning. After this the display link callback is never
  856              // invoked again, so `renderer.Thread` can free the `draw_now`
  857              // handle the callback points at.
  858              if (DisplayLink != void) {
  859                  if (self.display_link) |display_link| {
  860                      display_link.close();
  861                      self.display_link = null;
  862                  }
  863              }
  864  
  865              self.cells.deinit(self.alloc);
  866  
  867              self.font_shaper.deinit();
  868              self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
  869  
  870              self.config.deinit();
  871              self.api.deinit();
  872  
  873              self.* = undefined;
  874          }
n47 src/renderer/generic.zig:911-974
  911          /// Callback called by renderer.Thread when it exits. Called on the
  912          /// render thread. Releases all GPU resources before the API tears down
  913          /// its context, since after this the GL context will be gone.
  914          pub fn threadExit(self: *Self) void {
  915              {
  916                  self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  917                  defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
  918  
  919                  // Release swap chain and shaders. Shaders are only freed
  920                  // while unrealized and not every apprt unrealizes before
  921                  // destroying a surface (macOS never does), so mark it
  922                  // unrealized. This also stops any later draw.
  923                  self.display_realized = false;
  924                  self.releaseGpuResources();
  925  
  926                  // We don't release images in `releaseGpuResources`
  927                  // since it can be called whenever the terminal is
  928                  // occluded or unrealized, and we don't want to
  929                  // reupload images every time that happens.
  930                  self.images.deinit(self.alloc);
  931                  self.images = .empty;
  932  
  933                  if (self.bg_image) |img| {
  934                      img.deinit(self.alloc);
  935                      self.bg_image = null;
  936                  }
  937              }
  938  
  939              // If our API has to do things on thread exit, let it.
  940              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "threadExit")) {
  941                  self.api.threadExit();
  942              }
  943          }
  944

  946          pub fn loopEnter(self: *Self, thr: *renderer.Thread) !void {
  947              // If our API has to do things on loop enter, let it.
  948              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopEnter")) {
  949                  self.api.loopEnter();
  950              }
  951  
  952              // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
  953              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
  954  
  955              self.syncDisplayLink(null, &thr.draw_now);
  956          }
  957  
  958          /// Called by renderer.Thread when it exits the main loop.
  959          pub fn loopExit(self: *Self) void {
  960              // If our API has to do things on loop exit, let it.
  961              if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopExit")) {
  962                  self.api.loopExit();
  963              }
  964  
  965              // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
  966              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
  967  
  968              // Ask the display link to stop. The stop itself happens on
  969              // the main thread shortly after. `deinit` later stops the
  970              // link for certain before anything the display link callback
  971              // references is freed.
  972              const display_link = self.display_link orelse return;
  973              display_link.setRunning(false);
  974          }

n47 src/renderer/generic.zig:1244-1256
 1244          pub fn releaseGpuResources(self: *Self) void {
 1245              if (self.swap_chain) |*sc| {
 1246                  // Waits for any in-flight frames to complete, then
 1247                  // frees all GPU resources.
 1248                  sc.deinit();
 1249                  self.swap_chain = null;
 1250              }
 1251  
 1252              // Release the shaders as well if we're unrealized.
 1253              if (!self.display_realized) {
 1254                  self.shaders.deinit(self.alloc);
 1255              }
 1256          }
n47 src/renderer/generic.zig:1273-1298
 1273          fn syncDisplayLink(
 1274              self: *Self,
 1275              display_id: ?u32,
 1276              draw_now: ?*xev.Async,
 1277          ) void {
 1278              if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
 1279  
 1280              const display_link = self.display_link orelse display_link: {
 1281                  if (!self.config.vsync) return;
 1282                  const callback = draw_now orelse return;
 1283                  const result = DisplayLink.create(
 1284                      self.alloc,
 1285                      xev.Async,
 1286                      &displayLinkCallback,
 1287                      callback,
 1288                  ) catch |err| {
 1289                      // A locked macOS session can temporarily have no active
 1290                      // displays. Rendering continues without vsync and the
 1291                      // next call to this function tries again.
 1292                      log.warn("error creating display link; using fallback rendering err={}", .{err});
 1293                      return;
 1294                  };
 1295  
 1296                  self.display_link = result;
 1297                  log.info("created display link", .{});
 1298                  break :display_link result;
n47 src/renderer/DisplayLink.zig:120-146
  120  /// thread.
  121  ///
  122  /// The link is stopped before this returns, so the output callback is
  123  /// not invoked again and whatever `userdata` pointed at can be freed.
  124  ///
  125  /// No other method may be called on this link after or concurrently
  126  /// with `close`. In practice the render thread that makes those calls
  127  /// has already exited by the time the renderer is deinitialized.
  128  pub fn close(self: *DisplayLink) void {
  129      assert(!self.closed);
  130      self.closed = true;
  131  
  132      if (self.link.isRunning()) self.link.stop() catch |err| {
  133          log.warn("error stopping display link err={}", .{err});
  134      };
  135      self.link.release();
  136  
  137      // If a sync is queued on the main thread it runs after we return
  138      // and still needs this struct, so it frees it instead of us.
  139      if (!self.sync_queued.load(.seq_cst)) self.destroy();
  140  }
  141  
  142  fn destroy(self: *DisplayLink) void {
  143      const alloc = self.alloc;
  144      self.* = undefined;
  145      alloc.destroy(self);
  146  }
n47 src/renderer/DisplayLink.zig:205-219
  205  fn syncCallback(ud: ?*anyopaque) callconv(.c) void {
  206      const self: *DisplayLink = @ptrCast(@alignCast(ud.?));
  207  
  208      // Clear this before reading the requested state. See queueSync.
  209      self.sync_queued.store(false, .seq_cst);
  210  
  211      // If the owner closed the link while we were queued, it left the
  212      // struct for us to free. See close.
  213      if (self.closed) {
  214          self.destroy();
  215          return;
  216      }
  217  
  218      self.sync();
  219  }

  550          _ = posix.system.close(self.read_thread_pipe);
  551

n48 src/font/SharedGridSet.zig:411-431
  411  /// Decrement the ref count for the given key. If the ref count is zero,
  412  /// the grid will be deinitialized and removed from the map.j:w
  413  pub fn deref(self: *SharedGridSet, key: Key) void {
  414      self.lock.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  415      defer self.lock.unlock(global.io());
  416  
  417      const entry = self.map.getEntry(key) orelse return;
  418      assert(entry.value_ptr.ref >= 1);
  419  
  420      // If we have more than one reference, decrement and return.
  421      if (entry.value_ptr.ref > 1) {
  422          entry.value_ptr.ref -= 1;
  423          return;
  424      }
  425  
  426      // We are at a zero ref count so deinit the group and remove.
  427      entry.key_ptr.deinit();
  428      entry.value_ptr.grid.deinit(self.alloc);
  429      self.alloc.destroy(entry.value_ptr.grid);
  430      self.map.removeByPtr(entry.key_ptr);
  431  }
